1 
2330 台積電(TSMC)

報告日期： 2026/10/16 研 究 員：羅 金 鳳 (Sunny Lo ) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要：  
⚫ 4Q26營收展望符合預期，但毛利率受 N2良率調整問題而未有
顯著改善，不過因 N2 的出貨量快速大幅成長，有利未來整體
毛利率的改善。  
⚫ 積極擴充產能， N3將擴充三個新廠，且 N2的產能在 2027年
將擴充一倍，而後段的 Interposer 則與 Global  Foundry 進行
合作。  
⚫ AI五年的 CAGR >55%，不含 Agentic AI 所驅動的 CPU強勁
需求，顯見 AI的需求遠優於預期。  
⚫ 我們將 2026/2027 年的稅後 EPS由 106.63/142.76 元，上修
至 111.36/152.74 元。

一. 4Q26營收展望符合預期，但毛利率仍受 N2影響  
3Q26 AI因 AI持續帶動先進製程需求強勁， N3營收比重由 2Q26
的 30%上升至 33%，N2營收比重也由 2Q26 的 3%上升至 8%，
帶動台積電 3Q26 營收達 14,942 億元， QoQ +17.62 %，
YoY +50.95 %，在成本改善、高產能利用率 及製程的升級帶動下，
抵銷 2nm量產初期對毛利率的負影響下， 3Q26毛利率 66.41%，
達公司展望的上緣， 稅後 EPS 2 9.43元，優於預期。 4Q26在 AI
帶動的先進製程及先進封裝需求持續強勁下，預估 以美元對新台
幣 1:31.8 為基礎計算，預估 營收中 值約 16,949 億元，
QoQ+13.4 3%，YoY+ 62.02 %，符合市場預期，惟 仍受 N2量產良
率調整問題的影響，預估 4Q26 整體毛利率維持在 66%左右，，
單季稅後 EPS 32.81元，續創新高 。

二. AI需求強勁，將擴大投資及合作  
由於客戶及客戶的客戶持續釋出 強勁的需求，AI 需求 5 年(2025
～2030)的 CAGR將超越 55%，且此一數字不含 Agentic AI 所帶
動的 CPU需求，主因 CPU主要的應用除了 AI之外，亦應用於傳
統的 PC及非 AI領域，顯見 AI需求的延續性拉長且需求優於預
期，台積電將積極擴產滿足客戶需求，雖然 2026年資本支出維持
600～640億美元，但因持續的海內外擴產加上 2030 年在成本合
理及正面效益下， 會開始導入 Hi-NA EUV，因此，預估未來數年
的資本支出將大幅走高。  
投資評等：買進

     20 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：2440元 
     15.97 X 2027EPS

2

AI帶動 GPU、ASIC需求持續成長，但進入 Agentic AI 則是帶動
CPU需求強勁成長，驅動半導體榮景延續。由於目前需求遠遠大
過台積電所能供應的產能，且持續朝先進製程應用，因此，台積電
目前 N2已在新竹、高雄量產，且 2027年產能將會擴增一倍，應
用於智慧型手機、 HPC、AI，且 A14 將會在 2027 年進行風險性
試產，2028年量產； N3方面也會擴增三個新廠，台南廠的進度符
合預期， 1H27可量產，亞利桑那州第二個 Fab(N3)在 3Q27也可
開始量產 (比預期提前 )，熊本廠於 2028年裝機量產。 由於台積電
美國大客戶及美國政府 希望台積電在美擴大產能，因此，台積電將
會在亞利桑那州以外的德州擴產，會由台積電自已營運 Fab廠，
不過目前尚未有正式結論。 雖然看似擴充相當大的產能，但台積電
都已由上而上的評估且有紀律的擴產，在電力等關鍵指標方面也
都已考慮進去了。

除了先進製程需求強勁外，先進封裝的需求也相對緊俏，因此，台
積電在後段將與 Global Foundry 合作，由 Global Foundry 進行
Interposer 的生產，以滿足客戶需求 ，台積電則是衝刺利潤率更好
的先進製程。

三. 上修 2026/2027年稅後 EPS至 111.36 /152.74元 
2026年受惠 AI及 Agentic AI 需求超乎  預期的帶動下，台積電將
2026年全年的營收由 YoY+40%上修至 44%(以美元計價 )，因此，
我們預估台積電 2026 年營收 55,936 億元，YoY+46.85%，毛利
率 66.55%，稅後淨利 28,877 億元，YoY+68.1%，稅後 EPS由
106.63 元上修至 111.36元。2027年在 GPU、ASIC、CPU、XPU
需求持續強勁的帶動下， 台積電營運仍會是強勁成長的一年，我們
預估台積電 2027 年營收 77,218 億元， YoY+37.17%，毛利率
67.53%，稅後 EPS由 142.76 元上修至 152.74 元。

四. 投資建議維持買進，目標價 3055元 
在 AI需求強勁的帶動下， 先進製程產能持續緊俏，台積電除了積
極擴擴 N3產能外， 2027 年 N2的產能將增加一倍 ，在高階產品
應用上將取得高市佔率， 且在毛利率表現方面，因 先進製程領導地
位的穩固吸引更多訂單， 訂價策略的改變， 及良率的改善，帶動毛
利率維持高檔，帶動獲利 持續大幅成長， 因此，投資建議維持買
進，目標價 3055元(20X2027年稅後 EPS)。

貳、公 司 營 運 概 況

3 
一.公司簡介  
台積電(2330)成立於 1987年，為專業的 Fabless 半導體廠商，為
客戶生產的晶片廣泛地被運用在各種終端市場，例如行動裝置、高
效能運算、車用電子與物聯網等。 2019年，台積電及其子公司所
擁有及管理 約當年產能超過 1,200 萬片十二吋晶圓。在台灣設有
三座十二吋超大晶圓廠、四座八吋晶圓廠和一座六吋晶圓廠，並擁
有一家 100%持有之海外子公司 —台積電（南京）有限公司之十二
吋晶圓廠及二家 100%持有之海外子公司 —WaferTech 美國子公
司、台積電 (中國)有限公司之八吋晶圓廠。 台積電於 2011年第四
季開始導入 28nm，且開始加大先進製程技術的投資，於 2H12以
平面電晶體結構為基礎，在 20nm導入加強版的 HKMG 等技術，
並於 2014年進一步量產 16nm FinFET( 鰭式場效電晶體 )，持續推
進摩爾定律，儘管摩爾定律在 3nm以下將面臨物理極限，台積電
透過先進封裝製程延長摩爾定律，為高端產品 /客戶提供最先進的
技術。為因應美國製造優先及半導在在地化製造的需求，台積電於
2021年開始在美國亞利桑那州建廠，預計 興建六座先進製程廠、
2座先進封裝廠、 1個 R&D中心， 4nm已於 4Q24開始生產，預
估 3nm 也將在 2026 年進行量產，並且因為 AI 需求強勁的帶動
下，將加速 2nm及 16A的進廠速度，預估有機會於 2027～2028
年開始陸續量產。

二.營運概況及展望  
1、發展矽光子技術解決資料傳輸速度不足及訊耗損失問題  
10/2022 ChatGPT 推出後，其廣泛的推理能力，與使用者在各領
域以文字內容的方式對答如流，帶給使用者前所未有的體驗與 驚
豔，使用者呈現爆炸 性的成長，推升生成式 AI快速發展，使得 AI 
Server 呈現供不應求的現象 ，其不論在外觀的設計架構抑或 內部
的零組件規格甚至資料傳輸的頻寬及速度均較一般傳輸的 Server
大幅提升，且內部各元件的規格須同步提升才能使 AI Server 的強
大效能發揮最大的效用。

過往 Server 內部資料的傳輸採用銅線進行傳輸， 由於 AI Server
對資料傳輸的頻寬及傳輸速度的需求大幅提升， 銅線傳輸將產生
較大的訊號減損，使資料傳輸的品質不佳， 而以光做為資料傳輸的
解決方案，可解決資料傳輸訊號減損的問題， 因此，銅退光進的解
決方案勢在必行 。

矽光子的技術為銅退光進的最終解決方案， 但礙於技術及良率尚
不成熟，因此， CPO將為先行發展的技術，以 作為逐步達成光通
訊元件與主晶片直接在矽晶圓製程的階段即同時完成製作 的過渡
技術。在矽光子製作的過程中將以光波導取代 傳統銅線的傳輸，由

於光會沿著折射率較大的 物質前進，空氣的折射率為 1、水的折射
率為 1.33、玻璃的折射率為 1.46、二氧化矽的折射率為 1.6、矽
的折射率為 3.5，因此，光波導將選擇從矽晶圓上製作， 使光能進
行最佳的全反射，讓光能在光波導中的傳 送達最大的效率。由於矽
光子的發展為半導體未來的趨勢，大多數晶圓廠已投入研發，台積
電在此一趨勢下亦不缺席， 進行 40nm～90nm製程的矽光子技術
開發，預估 2025 年將利用 65nm 製程完成緊湊型通用光子引擎
(COUPE)的開發，支援可插拔式的連接器， 2026 年整合 CoWoS
封裝，將 CPO 與 Switch 晶片共同整合於同一基板上，完成共同
封裝光學 (CPO)的開發，未來將進一步將 CPO 與 XPU 共同整合
於矽仲介板上 ，實現晶片間更快的傳輸速度及較低的延時性。

圖一、矽光子發展示 意圖

資料來源： imec；宏遠投顧整理

2、透過 2.5D/3D 封裝延續摩爾定律  
矽為半導體重要材料，矽 1.0為平面式電晶 (Planar FET) 體時代，
由於電子遷移速度受到限制，製程節點 /閘極長度走到大約 28nm；
在 28nm以下進入到矽 2.0，為了改善電子元件漏電的問題， Intel
自 22nm 開始將平面世電晶體改成鰭式場效電晶體 (FinFET)，台
積電亦自 16nm開始將製程改為 FinFET，但當製程節點不斷往下
微縮至 5nm、3nm時，FinFET 將面臨摩爾定律的物理極限，即進
入矽 3.0時代，利用 2.5D及 3D封裝的技術可延續摩爾定律，台
積電在 2.5D封裝方面推出 CoWoS，Intel 推出 EMIB。CoWoS 在
晶片及基板之間加入 Si Interposer( 不具導電功能 )做為連接橋樑；
而 Intel 的 EMIB 則利用矽橋 (Silicon Bridge ；具有元件連通距離
短及反應速度快的特性 )做為多晶片之間連接的橋樑。

圖二、2.5D封裝示意圖

資料來源： Intel & 宏遠投顧彙整

在 3D封裝方面，台積電推出 3D Fabric 平台，Intel 推出 Foveros 
3D封裝架構。台積電 3D Fabric，主要為前段製程採用 Wafer on 
Wafer 及 Chip on Wafer 的 SoIC(系統整合單晶片 )方式，將晶片
進行 3D堆疊，然後結合後段先進封裝製程，包括 CoWoS及InFO，
將邏輯晶片及記憶體晶片進行 2.5D及 3D的封裝製程，達到縮小
IC 面積及提高效能的效果。而 Intel 的 Foveros 將不同製程節點
的晶片進行 3D封裝，包括 10nm製程的運算晶片、 22nm製程的
系統 I/O 晶片和 PoP(Package -on-Package)封裝的 DRAM 記憶
體。

圖三、3D封裝示意圖

資料來源： 網路 & 宏遠投顧彙整

### Page 2

Taiwan Electronics & Semiconductors
12 October 2026 Citi Research
2
PCB and substrate- 3Q26 was a strong quarter for substrate and CCL sectors, with
demand continuously piling up. Unimicron posted +23% QoQ sales growth in 3Q26,
beating Citi/consensus forecasts by 4%/6%. We expect 4Q26E ABF sales
momentum to be even stronger vs 3Q considering all the AI chips ramping up
together. CCL names also saw strong momentum across the board thanks to
enhanced product mix and benefit from price hike in 2Q26. We expect CCL sector to

see another round of price hikes from Nov onwards. For PCB, GCE’s Sept sales were
impacted by flooding in Thailand, which led to logistics disruption, while Tripod
posted record monthly sales backed by memory demand and early pull-in before
China Golden Week. With TPCA Show coming next week, we think
substrate/CCL/PCB sectors might see some event catalysts in the near term.
However, from a fundamental perspective, we still prefer substrate
(Unimicron>NYPCB>Kinsus) > CCL (EMC> TUC) > PCB.

PC/NB Supply chain - For NB ODMs, overall NB shipments see MoM increase in Sep
(+32% MoM) due to the quarter end inventory build; combined 3Q NB shipments
were down 17% QoQ due to  the early pull-in in 1H. We forecast 4Q26E NB shipments
to further decline -7% QoQ/-28% YoY given the muted demand on rising retails
prices and the increasing constraint in components procurement, While the price
hike effect in the NB products should partially offset shipment decline, we expect

increasing margins dilution impact to the NB ODM supply chains.
For lens components - Largan reported relatively muted sales results in Sep at
NT$5,779m (+15% MoM/-7% YoY); combined 3Q revenues are below market
estimates while Genius’ 3Q revenues were slightly better than expectation. Despite
the near-term sales performance, we see more upside opportunity coming from
their new development in optical business in the longer term. We expect Genius’
project with a leading US company on optical connector and ferrule to start

shipment in 1H27. For Largan, we expect it to benefit most from FA/FAU for CPO
market. We see its current engagement with the leading US client progressing
smoothly, as evidenced by its continued new land expansion for future capacity
build-out (see our recent report).
Thermal – Sep-26 sales as reported by thermal players (AVC & Auras) were up from
Aug at +6% MoM/+42% YoY. While AVC delivered another record month thanks to
solid ASIC demand and initial Vera Rubin shipments, Auras experienced MoM

decline due to client deferring pull-ins and Thailand flooding. Going forward, we
preliminarily expect both companies to see sequential revenue growth in 4Q26E,
driven by the AWS Trainium transition from air to liquid cooling, ramp-up of the TPU
and Vera Rubin projects.
Power – Delta reported MoM revenue recovery in Sep-26 (+1% MoM/+14% YoY), with
power electronics down 4% MoM. 3Q26 revenues were up 8% QoQ but below our
and Street’s expectation. We expect Delta to regain momentum with double-digit

QoQ growth in 4Q26E as ±400V enters the initial transition period, while in the long
term, HVDC is still progressing gradually while Delta maintains its technology
leadership. For BBU, we see Sep sales showing mixed momentum yet resilient BBU
demand remains intact. Dynapack’s Sep-26 sales declined 34% y/y and 54% m/m
to NT$776mn, reflecting 1) weaker PC shipments amid quarter-end inventory
adjustments and 2) deferred shipments of one BBU product, broadly consistent with

the near-term weakness flagged in our Aug-26 sales note. We expect a meaningful
sales rebound from Oct helped by shipments of new higher-power BBU products.
Meanwhile, AES maintained solid momentum with Sep-26 sales rising 25% y/y (+1%
m/m), suggesting resilient underlying BBU demand. Similarly, STL (4931.TWO; not
covered) delivered robust 176% y/y growth, further reinforcing our positive view on
the sector. We retain our constructive stance on BBU names, supported by

sustained demand and upcoming product ramps
Passive components - Yageo reported trended up monthly sales in Sep-26, as
demand in Europe and US recovered after the summer holiday, on top of sustained
AI demand. 3Q26 revenues were slightly above cons forecasts. Current quarter top
line still beats mgmt. 's "moderate growth" guidance. We believe that growth was
again broad-based across commodity and specialty products, and driven by both
volume and price. We expect a more visible ASP uplift in 4Q26E as specialty and

contract-based business could slow vs the spot and distributor channels.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Background: Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT), the world’s largest PCB 
manufacturer by revenue, generated revenue of TWD182.5bn in 2025. 
Historically focused on consumer electronics and smartphones, the 
company is shifting towards higher-value AI-server PCBs (HLC/iHDI), 
optical-module PCBs (mSAP), and IC substrates (ABF/BT). These 
businesses accounted for 21.6% of its revenue in 1H26, up from 11.7% in 
2025. Management targets this ratio to reach c.25% in 2026 and c.30% in 
2027. To support this shift, ZDT has 22 fabs under construction and plans 
to build nine more, primarily for mSAP/HDI, HLC, and ABF substrates.

Highlights: ZDT’s “One ZDT strategy” is gradually bearing fruit. Since 
2018, management has pursued its “One ZDT strategy” to broaden the 
product portfolio – including HLC, substrates, mSAP/HDI, rigid-flex and 
SLP – by leveraging its strengths in FPCB and consumer HDI. This 
strategy is beginning to pay off, and management expects its fast-growing 
segments, including mSAP HDI for optical transceivers, AI-server HDI and 
ABF/BT substrates, to gain momentum from this year. Management 
expects revenue from these segments to increase by c.150% YoY and 
account for c.25% of total revenue (or c.TWD50bn) in 2026, and to further 
increase by 100% YoY to TWD100bn in 2027. ZDT targets these fast-
growing segments to reach 30% of its revenue mix in 2027 and c.45-50% 
in 2030. Given current progress, the 2030 target could be reached in 2028. 
For mSAP HDI, management expects the 800G/1.6T transceiver product 
mix to shift to 7:3 in 2027, from 5:5 in 2026, and aims to become the largest 
supplier of optical-transceiver HDI in 2027. In AI-server HDI, we expect the 
2 leading ASIC vendors to gradually shift from pure HLC to thick HDI 
boards – a combination of HLC and HDI – in their next-generation designs, 
with ZDT a potential beneficiary. This could drive c.3x growth in ZDT’s 
business in 2027.

Product mix to support gross margin expansion. Margin expansion is 
increasingly driven by a favourable product mix. Gross margin reached 
22.4% in 1H26 (vs. 16.5% in 1H25). Management indicated that optical-
transceiver HDIs currently have the highest gross margin, at over 40%, 
followed by IC substrates, with ABF margins potentially reaching 50-60% 
and BT margins around 30% as a long-term target. Server HLC margins 
are around 20-30%, while HDI margins should be higher given their greater 
process complexity. With margins in traditional businesses at c.20%, ZDT 
expects growth in the newer segments to drive further margin expansion.

Valuation: The stock is trading at 36/19x PER for 2026/27E based on 
Bloomberg consensus. It’s past 3-year PER range is 10-30x.

20 October 2026

Business transformation bears fruit

➢ Key beneficiary of growth in optical transceiver & AI server HDI demand  
➢ Aggressive substrate capacity strategy paying off in current conditions   
➢ Margin expansion on fast-growing segments (HDI and substrates)

研 究  報 告                
晶呈科技(4768.TT) 
產業：化學工業 
氣體原料量產降本效應與玻璃加工產品放量，推動明年獲利高成⻑ 
 投資評價與建議 
考量三廠C4F6⼀級合成放量顯著優化⽑利率重回30%，且AI驅動TGV及CPO
加工產品步入規模化量產，⻑期動能強勁，參考台股半導體特化與先進封裝概
念股本益比30-35倍，因短期受高額研發費用投入、新廠折舊與轉虧為盈初期
致使當前本益比失真，故採用TGV產線滿載Forward PER。 
 重點評論分析 
特氣出貨回升，TGV量產放量：推估4Q26隨特氣出貨回升及客⼾去庫存告⼀
段落，營收可望季增73.7%至6.6億元，全年營收估增50.9%至18.6億元，玻璃
加工受惠AI高階封裝與CPO需求，GCB及One Piece Glass FAU已進入規模量
產，預估2H26營收較上半年成⻑230%，惟三廠延至4Q26試量產，特氣原料
成本改善遞延，⽑利率下修至23%，加上研發費用增加，使稅後EPS 0.29元。  
⼀級合成與高⽑利產品帶動獲利躍升： 預估2027年營收年增35.1%至25.1億
元，受惠AI ASIC大廠特氣需求放量、TGV及矽光子共同封裝相關應用擴產6倍
產能，並拓展生醫檢測市場，預計3Q27末相關產線陸續量產，Stripper隨認證
客⼾增加至9家，⽑利率因三廠C4F6⼀級合成與純化工廠預計1Q27放量，年
產能250噸，可降低外購原料成本，加上Stripper產能利用率提升，以及TGV、
生醫檢測及Stripper等高⽑利產品營收占比提升至20%，帶動年增7.92ppts至
30.9% ， 營益率升至12.5% ， 稅前盈餘年增936%至2.91億元 ， 稅後EPS 4.30元 。

### Page 2

2 
| 公司簡介  
公司所做的玻璃通孔技術(Through Glass Via ,TGV)主要應用於大積體電路玻璃載板(Glass Core Su bstrate, 
GCS)(非 Glass interposer)，是做為⼀個玻璃核心晶圓加工廠(TGV Glass Foundry)，並將加工好 的玻璃核心層
(Glass Core Board, GCB)，交付給下游 IC 載板廠或封測廠後，在玻璃核心的外側壓合 ABF 絕緣膜，並製作精細
的銅線路 ， 組合成完整的 GCS ． 亦透過玻璃加工技術開發⼀體成型式 CPO 光纖束導基座(One Piece Glass FAU)。 
GCB 是數個晶片叢共用的超大玻璃地基，支撐所有晶片和並保持封裝的平坦和穩定，解決穩定性(平坦度與結構支
撐)的瓶頸，位置在整個封裝體的最底層，玻璃材料解決熱膨脹、曲蹺度、以及對封裝尺寸的要求極限，目前公司
的製作玻璃微孔的貫穿深度與孔道寬度(孔徑)的比值已達到深寬比 100:1，全孔立體型態增圓度可達 99%、板厚
可達 1.85mm，單位面積產值相較 Glass Interposer 多 25-30 倍，在硬脆且易碎的玻璃材質上實現百倍深寬比，
是公司透過獨家氣態分解融蝕技術，在微觀垂直孔道上的極致控制力。 
One Piece Glass FAU 是⼀種應用於 CPO(共同封裝光學)與超高速光通訊系統中光電整合模組，超微細光纖束的
精準對位組裝，專為插裝與固定直徑僅 125 微米(µm)的超微細光纖束所設計(直徑極細，大約三根光纖才等於⼀
根頭髮的粗細)，在 AI 晶片架構從傳統電訊號傳輸轉向光訊號傳輸的過程中，該產品主要解決(1)微米級光纖對位
極難與光訊號損耗問題，公司雷射改質與氣態蝕刻技術(LADY)，在玻璃上打造出高⼀致性的微孔結構，提供高精
度的光纖對位，顯著提高光耦合效率(Optical Coupling Efficiency)，可減少多件式組裝產生的累積公差(2)高溫運
轉下的熱膨脹與光軸偏斜，利用玻璃材料極低熱膨脹係數的物理特性，確保光纖對位結構在⻑期高溫運算下仍保
持極佳的尺寸，(3)光軸穩定性以及光電整合元件的結構精密性，採用⼀體成型(One-Piece)的玻璃基座設計，結合
TGV 製程的高垂直度與高精度特性，配合客⼾在 AI 系統架構升級中對光電整合元件的規模化部署需求。 
圖表、Glass Core Board (左圖)不同象限與One Piece Glass FAU(右圖)顯微鏡下示意圖

資料來源：玉山投顧、公司資料 
| 營  運  概  況  
外銷與特氣出貨拉動累計營業收入倍增，受費用提升致⽑利率承壓：1H26 合併營收 8.21 億元，YoY+141%，主
要受惠特殊氣體外銷出口的大幅增加，氣體出貨支數達 13,022 支，其中 2Q26 營收 QoQ-13.9%至 3.8 億元，主
因在於部分外銷出口出貨量有所減少至 6,294 支，受韓國等記憶體客⼾要求調整出貨影響，但⽑利率
QoQ+11.1ppts 至 24.4%，因低⽑利產品(海外記憶體客⼾的特氣)相對 1Q26 出貨較少，1H26 ⽑利率 YoY-
20.45ppts 至 18.5%，除了產品組合關係，另因上游⼀級原料合成⾃給率尚未開出，使得原料成本負擔較沉重，
2Q26 費用率從 17.6%提升至 25.6%，主要來⾃研究發展費用大幅提升從 7.1%提升到 12.2%，目的在於為竹北五
廠、頭份三廠等新廠建置進行關鍵技術研發，1H26 稅前虧損 0.36 億元，稅後 EPS -0.99 元。 
特氣與 TGV 放量帶動 2H26 營收優於 1H26：3Q26 單季營收 3.8 億元，ＱoQ-0.6%，推估 3Q26 鋼瓶出貨量
6,000-6,200 支，主要來⾃ C4F6 內外銷出貨持續，⽑利率維持 24%，推估 4Q26 營收 QoQ+73.7%至 6.6 億元， 
4Q26 鋼瓶出貨量 7,500-7,800 支，2H26 鋼瓶出貨合計約 13,500-14,000 支，比 1H26 出貨量成⻑約 7.7%，隨
著全球半導體大廠的新產能與新廠陸續開出，帶動對氣體 C4F6 蝕刻氣體、Laser Gas 等的剛性需求持續增加，另

### Page 3

3 
外 2H26 隨客⼾去庫存化使需求回補，帶動氣體鋼瓶出貨量提升，並受惠於 AI 伺服器與高階封裝和矽光子共同封
裝(Co-Packaged Optics, CPO)，使玻璃加工市場快速成⻑，帶動相關特殊氣體的需求同步提升。 
玻璃加工技術在 TGV Glass Foundry 領域應用，已成功建置兩大可量產的核心產品線，配合年底 2 條產線(月產
能約 1 萬片)到位與量產放量，預估 2H26 營收相較 1H26 成⻑ 230%，主因為 GCB 與 One Piece Glass FAU 皆
已跨越工程驗證，正式進入單月接單與出貨片數達四位數以上的規模量產的放量階段，預估單片 150x150mm(5
萬孔/片)價值約$500-700 美元。 
特化產品 Stripper 目前設有⼀條年產能 288 頓產線，目前已被客⼾認證達 15 個品項，其中 7 個已達穩定交貨，
驗證通過的客⼾數已增加至９家，預估 2H26 營收相較 1H26 成⻑ 3%，加計其他產品，預估 2026 全年營收成⻑
率下修至 50.9%達 18.5 億元 ， 由於三廠(C4F6 ⼀級合成工廠)投產時程 ， 從先前 3Q26 延後至 4Q26 進行試量產 ，
使全年⽑利率從原先預估的 35%下修至 23%，加上研發費用擴張，下修稅前盈餘年成⻑ 41.3%至 0.28 億元，稅
後E P S  0 . 2 9元 。  
⼀級合成放量大幅優化特氣成本結構，TGV 導入 AI 大廠放量，帶動獲利大幅成⻑：邁入 2027 年，預估全年營收
成⻑ 35.1%至 25.1 億元，主要成⻑來⾃特殊氣體核心業務持續受惠 AI ASIC 大廠需求放量，另外玻璃加工應用於
AI 先進封裝與矽光子共同封裝產品預計於 3Q27 末累計建置達 12 條產線並正式量產，推動營收規模呈指數級成
⻑，在生醫檢測應用(竹北五廠)的最新進程上，已於生醫園區簽約購入竹北五廠房，將玻璃加工技術應用於生產生
醫用檢測儀器，在玻璃培養⽫微雕出高⼀致性的 3D 微孔陣列，模擬人體內真實的立體腫瘤微環境(3D 全立體型
態載具(Booster))，針對 EGFR 基因突變陽性的局部晚期非小細胞肺癌細胞進行精準控制併用標靶藥物泰格沙
(Tagrisso®)之細胞檢測實驗體外評估(In Vitro) ， 提升藥物對癌細胞的抑制與殺傷成效 ， 並藉由開發的生醫檢測儀
器，作為癌症精準用藥的關鍵數據，預計五廠將於 3Q27 末正式開始量產，此應用將使玻璃加工技術跨足高⽑利
生醫市場，現有廠房空間至少可規劃 6 條產線，後續將視客⼾需求進行滾動擴充。Stripper 隨 2026 年認證客⼾
增加朝向產能滿載，以及二廠無水氟化氫(AHF)量產線與三氯化硼(BCl3)試產線裝機完成，四條產線年產值上看 3-
4 億元的效益下，穩定貢獻營收。2027 全年⽑利率則受惠頭份三廠的 C4F6 ⼀級合成與純化工廠預計於 1Q27 正
式放量，設計年產能 250 噸，特氣⾃給率將大幅拉升，改變過去依賴外購二、三級純化原料的成本結構，使成本
大幅降低，加上特用化學品業務在產能利用率拉升，高⽑利產品如 TGV 玻璃載板、生醫檢測儀器、Stripper ⾃製
放量的佔比合計提升至 20%，使⽑利率顯著提升 7.92ppts 至 30.9%，費用率隨營業收入規模擴大而稀釋至 18.36%，
營益率提升 9.75ppt 至 12.5%，稅前盈餘年成⻑ 936%至 2.91 億元，稅後 EPS 4.30 元。

Document 180: 9月经济数据跟踪：经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月经济数据跟踪：经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速.pdf
Report source: 万联证券
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速

[Table_ReportType] 
——9 月经济数据跟踪

[Table_ReportDate] 2026 年 10 月 19 日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 事件：2026 年Q3,GDP 不变价同比4.5%，环比1.2%。工业增加值
同比由5.2%上行至5.5%，固投累计同比持平于-7.2%，其中制造
业、基建、房地产投资分别由-2.3%、-4%、-19.9%变动为-2.1%、
-4.2%、-20.6%，社零当月同比由0.4%上行至1.1%。 
⚫ 三季度GDP 略高于市场预期，全年经济目标有望达成。2026 年三
季度GDP 不变价同比增速为4.5%，高于市场预期的4.4%。第二、
三产业对GDP 同比拉动作用边际放缓，第一产业对GDP 同比拉动
作用加强。工业产能利用率环比上行，采矿业整体小幅回升，制
造业整体小幅回落，行业分化明显。 
⚫ 工业生产季节性回升，出口拉动作用走强，高技术产业生产高位
边际放缓。工业企业出口交货值大幅拉升，出口给部分行业生产
带来较强拉动。采矿业生产持续修复，石油开采业为主要拉动，
煤炭产量跌幅收窄。制造业生产整体小幅回落，上游制造业中，
非金属矿、黑色金属冶炼行业生产上行，极端天气影响消退，施
工旺季带来需求回补，政策性金融工具加速落地，带来行业边际
改善。化工在地缘政策不确定性影响下，企业补库需求提升，行
业生产上行，有色金属冶炼及压延工业、橡胶和塑料制品业在价
格走高等影响下，生产同比回落。中游制造业表现分化，整体仍
处高位，消费品制造业整体回落。下游制造业中，汽车、运输设
备、电子设备制造同比均回落，新质生产力生产边际放缓，新旧
动能分化收敛。 
⚫ 服务业生产指数回升，生产性服务表现仍强。“双节”给服务业
带来一定提振，服务业活动预期指数持平。商务活动指数上行，
信息传输、软件和信息技术服务业与租赁和商务服务业同比小幅
上行，租赁和运输业同比均有所修复。 
⚫ 投资止跌企稳，资金投放和落地稳步推进。随着稳增长政策逐步
落地，固定资产投资逐步止跌企稳。设备购置在政策推动下持续
上行，建安工程继续下跌，新开工偏弱，投资项目不足的影响仍
存。固投资金来源来看，今年以来债券累计同比维持高位，政府
专项债用于项目建设比重明显提升，预算内资金同比跌幅有所收
窄，项目资金逐步落地。 
⚫ 地产投资持续下滑，销售依然偏弱。地产新开工、竣工跌幅加
深，施工增速持平。地产销售“金九”行情表现偏弱，二手房销
售好于新房。一线城市和部分二线城市新房价格环比上行，三线
城市价格依然偏弱，二手房价格同比多数下跌，房价依然有待企
稳。9 月 29 日居民购房贷款贴息政策公布，有望给刚需带来一定
利好，政策效果仍有待观望。房企资金来源持续收紧，销售回款
和融资环境依然承压，房企拿地偏谨慎。四季度土地供应总量收
缩的特征预期持续，结构逐步改善。 
[Table_Authors] 分析师: 于天旭 
执业证书编号： S0270522110001 
电话： 17717422697 
邮箱： yutx@wlzq.com.cn

证券研究报告|宏观报告

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 2 页 共 13 页  
⚫ 基建投资持续放缓，制造业投资反弹上行，财政资金有望给四季
度增速带来支撑。三季度以来财政资金投放有所提速，但落地进
程依然偏慢，基建下行斜率明显放缓。随着8000 亿元政策性金融
工具、政府专项债和地方政府债务结存限额在四季度投放并落
地，“六张网”建设进程有望提速，四季度基建将发挥“压舱
石”的作用。制造业投资上行部分对冲了地产、基建下行的压
力，高技术产业增速边际放缓。大规模设备更新政策、出口强韧
性，以及稳增长政策逐步落地给制造业带来拉动。 
⚫ 消费增速修复， “双节”带来提振，结构表现分化。服务消费好
转，餐饮增速上行，线上消费小幅放缓。居民实际可支配收入边
际上行，人均消费支出持平，居民消费信心窄幅震荡，内需整体
依然偏弱。可选消费结构分化，汽车为主要拖累，地产下游消费
偏弱。金银珠宝类本月跌幅收窄最为显著，通讯器材类消费持续
走高，假期出行需求提振下，石油及制品类跌幅收窄。必选消费
整体表现稳健，粮油、食品类、日用品类上行幅度较大。 
⚫ 四季度政策将发挥托底作用，关注政策落地效果。9 月我国经济
外需拉动、生产强、内需弱的格局持续，但新旧经济分化程度边
际收敛。9 月稳增长政策逐步加码，政府债发行提速，在政策扶
持下，四季度经济有望缓步回升，但仍需关注财政资金向实物工
作量的转化效率，预期全年经济节奏呈现“U 型”走势。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，居民购房持续低迷，我国
政策出台和落地不及预期

事件 
2026年三季度，GDP不变价同比增速4.5%， 季调环比增长1.2%， 前值0.9%。9月，工业
增加值当月同比增速上行0.3个百分点至5.5%，环比上涨0.61%，前值为0.54%。前三
季度，固定资产投资累计同比增速-7.2%，8月为-7.2%，其中，房地产开发投资累计
同比增速回落0.7个百分点至-20.6%，基建投资累计同比增速回落 0.2个百分点至-
4.2%， 制造业投资增速累计同比上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%。 社会消费品零售总额同比
增速由上月的0.4%上行至1.1%，

1    三季度经济增速略高于市场预期 
三季度GDP增速略高于市场预期，外需强劲拉动生产为主要支撑，全年经济目标有望
达成。2026年三季度GDP不变价同比增速为4.5%，市场预期为4.4%，一、二季度同比
增速分别为5%、4.3%；三季度环比增速为1.2%， 高于前值0.9%， 前三季度累计同比为
4.6%。分产业看，第一、二、三产业累计同比增速分别由上半年的3.7%、3.9%、5.2%
变动为3.7%、3.8%、5.2%，仅第二产业同比增速小幅放缓。第二、三产业对GDP同比
拉动作用边际放缓，第一产业对GDP同比拉动作用加强。二季度以来，地缘政治扰动
给全球经济、 物价带来较大风险， 我国出口保持强劲，给工业生产带来拉动， 为经济
重要支撑。 原油等大宗商品涨价压力加大， 输入性通胀被动抬升，我国工业品价格上
涨，CPI总体表现温和。 
工业产能利用率环比上行，行业分化明显。工业产能利用率小幅上行至73.1%，二季
度为73%。分行业来看，采矿业整体小幅回升，但仍低于去年同期，其中煤炭开采和
洗选环比上行幅度较大， 石油和天然气开采业小幅回落。电力、 热力、 燃气及水生产
和供应业环比回升。制造业整体小幅回落， 上游制造业中， 非金属矿物制品、 化学原
料及化学制品业产能利用率环比修复， 有色、 黑色金属冶炼及压延产能利用率同环比
下滑。中游专用设备制造产能利用率环比上行，同比高于去年同期， 通用设备制造环
比放缓，但仍处于高位，电气机械和器材制造环比上行明显。下游制造业表现分化，
计算机、 通信和其他电子设备产能利用率由78.7%上行至80.2%， 领跑其他行业； 汽车
制造业同、环比下跌幅度较大，医药制造业也有所回落。

2    工业生产回升，出口拉动作用较强 
季末月生产季节性回升， 出口拉动作用走强， 采矿业持续修复， 高技术产业生产高位
边际放缓。9月， 工业增加值当月同比增速上行0.3个百分点至5.5%，与市场预期的5.5%
一致，前三季度累计同比增速为5.3%。9月环比上涨0.61%，前值为0.54%。发电量同
比由上月的-0.8%回落至-1.2%。工业企业出口交货值当月同比由上月的11.1%上行至
17%，规模以上工业企业产销率当月同比由-0.1%下行为-0.2%。分三大门类看，9月，
采矿业同比上行2.7个百分点至1.3%，石油和天然气开采业本月同比上行幅度相对最
大，原煤产量同比跌幅收窄，天然原油产量同比上行，天然气产量同比由正转负。制
造业同比由上月的6.1%回落至6%； 高技术产业同比由上月的16.7%回落至15.3%。 电力、
燃气及水的生产和供应业同比上行0.7个百分点至5.6%。

图表3：采矿业持续修复，高技术产业高位放缓（%） 图表4：工业企业出口交货值增速攀升（亿元，%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

化工、 黑色金属行业生产上行， 下游制造业生产整体放缓， 消费品制造业整体回落。
细分行业来看， 上游制造业中，化学原料及化学制品制造业增速持续上行， 中东地缘
政治给供应链和原料价格带来较大扰动， 不确定性下企业补库需求提升， 乙烯、 化纤
产量上行。 非金属矿物制品、黑色金属冶炼及压延工业同比修复上行， 钢材、 水泥产
量同比跌幅收窄，极端天气影响消退后， 施工旺季带来需求回补， 政策性金融工具加
速落地， 叠加原材料成本上涨带来行业边际改善。 有色金属冶炼及压延工业、橡胶和
塑料制品业同比回落， 价格高位给生产带来一定抑制。中游制造业表现分化， 整体仍
处高位， 其中，通用设备、电气机械及器材制造业同比回落，专用设备制造业同比上
行。下游制造业中，汽车制造业同比回落2个百分点至6.7%，下滑幅度较大，高基数
带来一定影响；铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业同比回落1.7个百分点
至11.7%，增速持续放缓。计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业同比回落，工业机器
人产量同比下滑幅度较大，集成电路产量同比下行，微型电子计算机产量跌幅收窄。
消费品制造业中，医药制造业同比回落0.3 个百分点至 4.9% ， 农副食品加工业、食品
制造业生产增速放缓，纺织业同比小幅上行。

服务业生产指数回升， “双节”带来一定提振，商务活动也有所修复，生产性服务表
现仍强，服务业活动预期指数持平。服务业生产指数9月当月同比为4.8%， 较前值4.1%
显著上行， 中秋节和十一长假给服务业带来一定提振， 前三季度累计同比为4.7%，与

受到信心因素制约。 商务活动指数当月同比由49.3%上行至50.2%，信息传输、 软件和
信息技术服务业与租赁和商务服务业同比小幅上行，租赁和运输业同比均有所修复，
生产性服务依然相对强势。

3    投资：地产、基建延续磨底，制造业增速边际修复 
投资增速止跌企稳，资金投放和落地稳步推进，设备购置在政策推动下持续上行， 建
安工程继续下跌。2026年前三季度， 固定资产投资累计同比增速-7.2%，8月为-7.2%，
高于市场预期的-7.5%。固投季调环比增速为0.07%， 前值为-0.55%。随着稳增长政策
逐步落地， 固定资产投资逐步止跌企稳。其中， 房地产开发投资累计同比增速继续回
落0.7个百分点至-20.6%；基建累计同比增速回落0.2个百分点至-4.2%，制造业累计
同比增速上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%，民间固定资产投资回落0.3个百分点至-10.4%。
设备工器具购置同比增速由9.3%上行至9.7%，设备更新政策效果持续显现；建筑安装
工程同比由-9.8%回落为-10%，新开工偏弱，投资项目不足的影响仍存。第二产业投
资增速上行至主要支撑， 第三产业投资增速小幅回落， 第一产业下滑幅度较大。固投
资金来源来看，今年以来债券累计同比维持高位， 政府专项债用于项目建设比重明显
提升，预算内资金同比跌幅有所收窄，项目资金逐步落地。

3.1  房地产投资持续下滑，销售依然偏弱 
房地产投资增速持续下探，新开工、竣工跌幅加深。2026年9月，房地产投资累计同
比增速继续回落0.7个百分点至-20.6%，具体来看，房屋新开工面积累计同比增速回
落0.1个百分点至-24.9%，房屋施工面积累计同比持平于上月的-12.8%，竣工增速由
-23.7%回落为-25.1%。 
地产销售“金九”行情表现偏弱，二手房销售好于新房。9月，商品房销售面积累计
同比由-12.1%继续下探至-13.2%， 商品房销售额由-13%下跌为-13.9%， 房地产销售 “量”
的跌幅相对 “价” 更大。从70城房价数据来看， 一线城市新房和二手房销售价格环比
上涨， 二手房价格同比多数下跌，仅上海同比上行。 部分二线城市新房销售价格环比
上行， 三线城市新房、 二手房价格同环比持续放缓， 房价依然有待企稳。二手房交易
网签面积累计同比由10.6%小幅上行至10.7%， 二手房销售好于新房的格局持续， 但二
手房交易景气难以传导至房企回款与投资。商品房待售面积累计同比由-1.1%下跌至
-1.5%， 其中住宅累计同比由-0.5% 下行至 - 0.7% ， 地产库存持续去化。 8 月地产政策陆
续出台， 北京、 上海等核心城市购房调节进一步放松。 地产直至竣工验收具备交付条
件后方可收取购房资金和按揭贷款，优化了居民的购房环境，但从9月数据来看提振
效果相对有限。9月29日居民购房贷款贴息政策公布， 政策在10月1日期实施， 有望给

刚需带来一定利好，政策效果仍有待观望。 
图表10：房屋新开工、竣工增速持续下探（%） 图表11：商品房销售持续回落，库存保持去化（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 
房企资金来源持续收紧， 销售回款和融资环境依然承压。房地产开发资金来源增速由
-21%回落为-21.6%。其中，个人按揭贷款跌幅小幅收窄，二手房交易活跃带来支撑，
定金及预收款增速与上月持平， 地产 “金九” 行情偏弱， 地产销售回款受限。 国内贷
款跌幅小幅修复上行， 自筹资金增速进一步下滑，地产行业信贷投放保持审慎， 房企
资金来源持续承压。 
房企拿地同比持续回落， 去库背景下， 房企依然偏谨慎。9月，100大中城市成交土地
占地面积环比季节性上行， 但同比由-16.1%继续小幅下调至-16.8%，房企拿地依然集
中在一线城市与核心城市的优质地块。房地产仍处去库进程当中，预售资金制度改革
影响将逐步显现， 房企资金周转受到约束， 叠加销售端和资金来源的限制，四季度土
地供应总量收缩的特征预期持续，结构逐步改善。 
图表12：房企资金来源持续承压（%） 图表13：土地成交面积同比延续低位（万平方米，%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

3.2  基建增速持续放缓，财政资金落地有望给行业带来托举 
基建投资持续放缓，财政资金有望给四季度增速带来支撑。 9 月， 基建累计同比增速
继续回落0.2个百分点至-4.2%， 三季度以来财政资金投放有所提速， 基建下行斜率明
显放缓， 但落地进程依然偏慢。 随着8000亿元政策性金融工具、 政府专项债和地方政
府债务结存限额在四季度投放并落地， “六张网” 建设进程有望提速， 四季度基建将

发挥“压舱石”的作用。 
基建分项整体表现偏弱， 公共设施管理业下滑幅度较大。从细分项来看，水利、 环境
和公共设施管理业跌幅进一步加深， 且下滑幅度最大。交通运输、 仓储和邮政业增速
持续下探， 但下行速度边际放缓。 电力、 热力、 燃气及水的生产和供应业增速小幅修
复上行，但仍为负增，随着高基数效应减弱，增速有望边际修复。

3.3  制造业投资反弹上行，高技术产业增速放缓 
制造业投资反弹上行，高技术产业增速边际放缓，出口和政策为主要拉动。9月，制
造业累计同比增速上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%，部分对冲了地产、 基建投资下行的压力，
民间固定资产投资回落0.3个百分点至-10.4%，依然表现偏弱；高技术产业累计增速
由5.2%回落至4.9%，设备工器具购置同比增速由9.3%上行至9.7%。 大规模设备更新政
策、 出口强韧性， 以及稳增长政策逐步落地给制造业带来拉动，上游资源品、 关键材
料等自主补库需求提高，也给制造业带来支撑。四季度随着财政资金投放陆续落地，
给制造业的支撑效果有望逐步显现。

图表16：制造业投资反弹，与存货走势小幅收敛（%） 图表17：出口韧性给制造业投资带来支撑（%）

4    消费增速修复，结构表现分化 
消费增速修复上行， “双节”带来提振，服务消费好转，线上消费放缓，内需整体依
然偏弱。9月， 社会消费品零售总额同比增速由上月的0.4%上行至1.1%， 市场预期0.7%，
累计同比持平于上月的1.1%。其中， 除汽车以外的消费品零售增速由上月的2.5%上涨
为3.9%， 上行幅度大于整体； 餐饮同比由上月的1.1%上涨为2.1%； 限额以上企业零售
同比由上月的-3.7%上涨为-2.1%。实物商品网上零售累计同比由4.3%回落为3.8%，网
上商品和服务占社零比重高位小幅下滑。城镇消费同比由0.2%上行至0.8%， 农村消费
由1.6%回升至2.7%，两者差距再度走扩。 
居民实际可支配收入边际上行， 人均消费支出持平，居民消费信心依然偏弱。2026年
前三季度， 全国居民人均可支配收入名义累计同比增长5.3%， 上半年为5.2%；扣除价
格因素实际累计同比4.4%， 前值为4.2%； 居民人均消费支出累计同比持平于上半年的
3%。从数据更新至8月的消费者信心指数来看，居民消费信心和意愿均小幅上行，但
收入信心指数持续回落。居民存款缓步回落， 就业和收入压力下， 信心有待进一步修
复。 
图表18：居民存款高位缓步回落（%） 图表19：消费者信心指数上行，收入信心指数回落

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

可选消费结构分化， 汽车为主要拖累， 地产下游消费偏弱， 必选消费表现稳健。可选
消费中，金银珠宝类本月跌幅收窄最为显著，黄金价格持续回落，高基数效应走弱，
给增速带来一定拉动。地缘政治影响下， 油价持续走高，在中秋和十一出行需求提振
下， 石油及制品类跌幅收窄。 汽车消费跌幅相对最大， 消费补贴和税费优惠政策退坡
带来一定影响。 地产下游行业中， 家电增速小幅放缓， 仍保持正增， 家具、 建材类跌
幅明显收窄。消费补贴对耐用品消费的透支作用逐步显现， 居民消费信心偏弱， 汽车、
地产后周期产品消费持续承压。 通讯器材类在高基数下， 增速持续走高， 新品发布周
期，内存价格大涨拉升了手机售价。必选消费增速均修复上行，其中粮油、食品类、
日用品类上行幅度较大。

5    失业率季节性回落，结构压力仍存 
失业率季节性回落， 农民工就业压力加大。9月，城镇调查失业率由5.3%下行至5.2%，
31个大城市城镇失业率也由5.3%下行至5.2%，外来农业户籍人口失业率持平于8月的
5.0%，BCI企业招工前瞻指数边际回落。9月就业人员平均工作时间由48.2小时/周上
行至48.3小时/周。从年龄结构来看，更新至8月的失业率数据中，16-24岁的年轻劳
动力失业率持续走高，25-29岁也有所上行，大学生毕业季带来的影响持续。随着大
学生就业逐步被吸纳，9月毕业季带来的影响季节性减弱。

6    总结与展望 
四季度政策将发挥托底作用，关注政策落地效果 。9月我国经济外需拉动、生产强、
内需弱的格局持续，但新旧经济分化程度边际收敛。9月稳增长政策逐步加码，政府
债发行提速， 在政策扶持下， 四季度经济有望缓步回升， 但仍需关注财政资金向实物
工作量的转化效率，预期全年经济节奏呈现“U型”走势。

7    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，居民购房持续低迷，我国政策出台和落地不及预期。

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

Governor Hauser's fireside chat. Offshore,
China GDP was the key release. "

5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,
as we expected, though the mix highlights that the waning in
momentum is uneven, centred on investors rather than owner-
occupiers. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of
strength, notwithstanding some slowing recently.
For September, we expect credit growth to nudge to 0.5%mth,
with the divergence between housing credit and business
credit persisting.
AUS: Q3/Sep CPI (%qtr/%mth)

Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.6%, Westpac f/c: 1.3%,
Market f/c: 1.3%, Range: 0.6 to 1.4.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.4%, Westpac f/c: 0.6%,
Market f/c: 0.6 Range: 0.3 to 0.8
As noted in the review of the August CPI, our September
quarter CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr holding the
annual pace flat at 3.9%yr. At the margin there was a 0.04ppt
increase due to a lift in auto fuel but it still rounds to 1.3%.
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% highlighting that part

of the increase is due to usual seasonality.
In the month, the September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% with
the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Recreation & culture make
the largest contribution but transport is not far behind. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate increases 0.5%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.
AUS: Q3/Sep Trimmed Mean (%qtr/mth)
Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.9%,
Market f/c: 1.0%, Range: 0.9 to 1.0.

Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.3%,
Market f/c: 0.3%, Range: 0.2 to 0.4
Our September quarter Trimmed Mean estimate is unchanged
at 0.9%qtr with the annual pace dipping a touch from 3.6%yr
to 3.5%yr. There was a small downward revision of 0.03ppt to
0.88% compared to our original published estimate but it still
rounds to 0.9%qtr.
In the month of September we are looking for a 0.3% in
the Trimmed Mean holding the annual pace steady at 3.6%

which will be the fourth consecutive month at this pace. The
distribution of the September Trimmed Mean ranges from
–0.3% to 0.7%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation
picture will probably be judged to have worsened slightly
given recent movements in energy prices and the exchange
rate. A vote seems very possible as differences in view will
likely remain among the MPC about how much the medium-
term inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.
Forward guidance will likely leave open the option to raise

the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy Statement
without presenting it as a sure thing.
NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

### Page 9

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.9
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade:
Rapid advances in Artificial Intelligence (AI) have prompted prominent architects of
the new economy to conclude the Singularity is arriving ahead of schedule as signs
of its effects proliferate in the multitude of new AI models running in the increasing

number of data centers driving one of the biggest investment booms in U.S. history.
Superintelligence that surpasses collective human intelligence has long been one of the
predictions of futurists analyzing the implications of the Singularity. Superabundance is
one of the results of Superintelligence predicted by those who have written about the
consequences of this major acceleration in technological innovation. The early arrival of
these radical forces for change poses imminent challenges for society and investors that

will shape the next decade in critical ways.
In particular, the heightened uncertainty about how this new era plays out increases the
utility of a well-diversified approach to wealth management in our view.
Market View—Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite: The recent
rise in Treasury yields reflects a confluence of factors, including Federal Reserve (Fed)
policy uncertainty, deteriorating fiscal dynamics, resilient economic growth, persistent

inflation concerns and a record wave of debt issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate borrowers, particularly in the long end of the curve. Importantly, we view this
as a period of market adjustment rather than a cause for alarm.
Episodes of debt pressures can be a natural consequence of elevated supply, in addition
to other external factors and could be resolved through higher yields that help balance
supply and demand. While interest rate volatility may remain elevated and long-term

yields could settle at structurally higher levels, today's combination of attractive nominal
and real yields presents a potential opportunity for investors to extend beyond cash and
short-duration holdings and move closer to strategic duration targets.
The Ticker Tape—Top Questions Answered Directly by Our Chief Investment
Officer (CIO).

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications
Rapid innovation, political upheaval
and increasing creative destruction
raise the difficulty of valuing
longer duration assets as we saw
earlier this year in the software
space. Higher-risk premia are a
likely result. On balance, this favors

preference.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investmen t Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in
yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to concerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to
other Go

vernment bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason f
or yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still abo
ve average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.
