Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Exhibit 2: Charting the “Yentervention” and U.S. Treasury Yields Surge Across the Curve.
A) The Yen Regains Some Strength. B) 5-Year Forward Yields Hold Above 5%.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.
Investment Implications
While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings
growth, robust consumer demand
(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

A) Net Foreign Purchases of US Equities and Investment Funds. B) FDI Inflows to the U.S.

is bullish for U.S. securities, both near-

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

Document 310: Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 1: Rising yields and uncertainty have increased the cost of capital 
30-year government bond benchmark yields (%) 
          













### Page 2

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

4. But despite the rise in yields, nominal GDP and profit growth remain strong and, until 
recently, the impact of rising bond yields has been offset by the strength in corporate 
profits. Earnings growth has been the main driver of equity returns over the past 18 
months in all regions (Exhibit 5). This strength has meant that PE multiples have either 
been flat (Japan and Europe) or have fallen (the US, Asia and EM).

5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

while memory and chips stocks have also experienced lower multiples as their earnings

surge. Globally technology has seen its PE ratio moderate, falling below its 20-year 
median (Exhibit 10). Meanwhile, the spending of these companies has spilled over into 
better earnings and more visible cash flows in many other parts of the economy. 
Healthcare, Consumer Discretionary and Industrials are all more expensive in absolute 
terms than IT, and Industrials has re-rated and now has a PE close to the 90th percentile 
of its 20-year range.

9. For these reasons, we have argued that Technology equities have not experienced a 
classic bubble because valuation levels are much lower than in previous financial 
bubbles. Nevertheless, the risk for investors is less about valuation and more whether 
there is an unsustainable earnings bubble. Bubbles in earnings, rather than valuations, 
have occurred in previous periods.  The Bank sector, for example, briefly became the 
biggest sector in the S&P 500 in the run up to the financial crisis of 2008/09 (Exhibit 11). 
Unlike the technology bubble of the late 1990s, or the Japanese bubble of the late 
1980s, Bank stocks did not experience a major valuation bubble at the time, but the 
surge in earnings that drove the sector’s outperformance proved to be unsustainable.

Exhibit 10: Industrials now trade at higher valuations than Technology

### Page 8

There are, however, important differences to the Tech sector today. The earnings of the 
banks were boosted by sharply rising leverage and the asset that they were financing – 
US real estate – did experience a valuation bubble. When the housing market collapsed, 
pushing economies into recession, it triggered a sharp fall in underlying earnings for the 
banking sector. There are important differences today. First, technology profits remain 
very robust, and balance sheets are strong overall. Second, interest coverage ratios for 
the aggregate S&P 500 and its median stock, for example, rank in the 99th and 68th 
percentiles relative to the past 20 years. Third, demand for compute is accelerating and 
outstripping supply; Microsoft recently declared their intent to triple data centre 
capacity in 6 years. The bottlenecks are increasingly power, land and data centre 
capacity rather than a lack of compute demand. At our annual Communacopia 
Technology Conference, Nvidia reiterated its 2030 AI TAM outlook to $3tn-$4tn and 
supply constraints continue to be broad based. Nonetheless, any slowdown in profit 
growth, in an environment of a much higher cost of capital, could put downward 
pressure on equity prices, reducing confidence in future cash flows across the ecosystem 
from the hyperscalers to the ‘pick and shovels’ that have been benefiting from the capex 
boom.  
10. Given the near-term risks in bond markets, we remain neutral on equities, alongside 
other asset classes on a 3-month horizon, but continue to be overweight equities over 
12 months. While profit growth is likely to slow from the recent strong pace, ongoing 
growth in nominal GDP should underpin equity markets and the earnings cycle 
propelling equity returns even without any valuation expansion from current levels and 
our economists continue to see a low risk of recession. As our asset allocation team has 
shown, earnings growth and ROE are likely to continue to be the key drivers of equity 
performance relative to bonds, and we expect these fundamentals to remain supportive 
for equity returns. Nevertheless, we continue to recommend well diversified equity 
exposure as opportunities continue to broaden across geographies, sectors and factors.

### Page 9

Stock correlations are at multi-year lows across the major equity markets (Exhibit 12).

Even within sectors like technology, correlations have fallen sharply (Exhibit 13). The 
‘magnificent seven’ that was long seen as a homogeneous group of technology winners 
has also seen significant dispersion of returns. Differentiation, diversification and alpha 
generation are likely to remain the key drivers of equity returns. With higher bond yields, 
valuations are not likely to rise making earnings the critical driver of returns for investors.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
Markets were generally subdued on Tuesday. US equities
bucked the broader sell-off, supported by solid earnings
despite renewed concerns around the AI trade. Oil prices
softened and long-dated Treasury yields edged lower

following unconfirmed reports that Qatar and Pakistan had
proposed a framework to revive negotiations, although the
reports were later denied and much of the initial market
reaction unwound.
• US equities proved resilient, with strong earnings and
a modest decline in oil prices offsetting broader market
weakness. The S&P 500 gained 0.3%, while the Dow
Jones outperformed, rising 1.0%, and the NASDAQ was
unchanged. Other regions fared less well, with the Euro

Stoxx 50 down 0.4% and the FTSE 100 falling 0.3%.
• The previous day’s tech-led rebound in the US failed to carry
over to Asian markets, with the region experiencing a broad
sell-off. Elevated oil prices and borrowing costs continued to
weigh on sentiment, compounded by renewed AI concerns
ahead of key earnings releases in the US. The KOSPI led
declines, falling 3.7%, while the Nikkei 225 and Hang Seng
both lost 1.3%. Locally, the ASX 200 followed the regional

trend, declining 0.3% after paring deeper losses during
afternoon trade.
• The US Treasury yield curve flattened, with the 2-year yield
rising 1bp and the 10-year yield falling 3bps, with softer
oil prices contributing to the move lower at the long end.
Futures markets assign just a 17% probability to an October
hike, while a December move remains fully priced, taking
the implied year-end fed funds rate to 4.14%. Long-dated
European bond yields also moved lower as oil prices eased,

with the 10-year gilt yield down 2bps and the 10-year Bund
yield falling 6bps.
• Australian government bond yields were little changed, with
the 3-year yield rising 1bp and the 10-year yield gaining
2bps. Swap markets remain unconvinced of additional RBA
tightening in 2026, with the probability of a November rate
hike priced at around one in four, taking the implied year-end
cash rate to 4.73%.
• The US dollar eased, with the DXY down 0.3%. The AUD

regained its 70 handle against the greenback, rising 0.3%
to 0.7001. Other major currencies also gained against the
USD, with the yen up 0.1% and the euro rising 0.2%, while
sterling outperformed, gaining 0.6% following a more
resilient unemployment reading.
• Brent was little changed overall as steep earlier losses on
unconfirmed reports of renewed peace negotiations were
largely unwound later in the session. Brent ended 0.4%
lower at US$109/bbl, while WTI maintained more of its

earlier decline, falling 1.8% to US$102/bbl. The premium
for prompt barrels continued to ease from recent highs, with
Dated Brent trading at US$118/bbl. Gold declined 0.5%,
while copper gained 0.6%, aluminium fell 0.5% and iron ore
declined 0.4%.
International Data
US housing starts declined 5.2% in September after a
downwardly revised 5.9% fall in August (prev -2.6%), returning
near its 5-year low. Building permits edged 0.2% lower in
September after a 2.1% loss in August. In the established home

market, pending home sales dropped 7.3% in September to
be 12.6% lower over the year. Serviceability and cost-of-living
continue to weigh heavily on the housing market.
The Euro Area ZEW survey of expectations fell from 25.8
to 18.5 in October under the weight of high oil prices and
increased uncertainty over the near-term economic outlook.
Still, the current level is above the average of the past 5 years,
and only modestly below the full history average of 21.4.

The UK ILO unemployment rate edged down from 4.9%
to 4.7% in the 3 months to August as employment growth
strengthened from 66k to 102k on a 3 month average basis.
Local Data
We published our October Commodities Update, covering
recent market developments and forecasts across the broader
commodities complex (see here).

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
2
8 
September 
2026

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

Document 407: US SECTORS CALL_ Tech Wrecked, Health Care & Staples Rally

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Tech Wrecked, Health Care & Staples Rally.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US SECTORS CALL: Tech Wrecked, Health Care & Staples Rally
Date:    7 hours ago
US SECTORS CALL: Tech Wrecked, Health Care & Staples Rally

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US SECTORS CALL: Tech
Wrecked, Health Care & Staples
Rally
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

The S&P 500 fell 2.0% this week, and Information Technology (MW) took
the biggest hit. The sector fell 6.4%, the worst of the 11 sectors (chart).
The iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) fell 11.8%. Credit worries spread
to AI infrastructure. CoreWeave fell 25% last week. Its worst day was
Wednesday, when it sank 15% as its bond yields soared. It was the IT
sector's second straight losing week. Industrials (OW) also fell for a
second week. On a ytd basis, Energy is up 52.7%, the best of the 11
sectors. IT is second at 21.6% (chart).

### Page 2

Consumer Staples (UW) and Energy (OW) led the 11 sectors, each up
5.0%. Health Care (OW) rose 3.4%. Every defensive sector gained, with
Utilities (MW) up 2.0% and Real Estate (UW) up 1.2%. Health Care and
Staples rallied for different reasons. Earnings drove Health Care's gains.
Staples' gains have been driven by its multiple.
Here's more on Information Technology, Health Care, and Consumer
Staples:
(1) Information Technology. The tech selloff last week was led by

Semiconductor Equipment (down 17.4%) and Communication Equipment
(down 13.3%) (chart). IT Consulting & Other Services rose 5.6%, and
Technology Hardware slipped only 1.5%.

### Page 3

Two weeks ago, Semiconductor Equipment was the best-performing IT
industry, up 97.3% ytd. It is now up 51.6%. Its forward P/E has fallen to
27.0 from 31.1. Its earnings outlook hasn't changed. Analysts still expect
growth of 35.0% this year and 48.5% next year (chart).
Semiconductor Equipment and Communication Equipment are the only IT
industries for which analysts expect faster growth next year (chart).
The selling hit the expensive industries. Semiconductor Equipment trades

at 27.0 times forward earnings and Communication Equipment at 25.2.
Semiconductors, the biggest contributor to IT's earnings, trades at a 14.7
multiple, below the S&P 500's 18.9 (chart). That is less than half its June
2024 peak of 35.5.

### Page 4

A low multiple can be a warning signal for a cyclical industry. Investors
tend to pay the least when earnings are peaking and the most when they
bottom. The industry's forward P/E reached 79.1 in February 2009, as
earnings collapsed. So far, analysts are still raising their estimates. They
expect earnings to grow 114.0% this year and 77.5% next year. As long as
estimates keep rising, we see this week's semiconductor selloff as a
buying opportunity. We retain our market weight rating on Information

Technology.
(2) Health Care. Health Care rose 3.4% this week, and every industry
gained. Health Care Distributors rose 16.1%. Even Health Care
Equipment, down 16.3% ytd, rose 4.4%.
Earnings are doing the heavy lifting. In August, forward earnings and the
forward P/E contributed about equally to the sector's ytd gain, at 6.6% and
6.3%, respectively. Forward earnings is up 9.2% ytd, while the forward P/E
is up only 2.8%. The price index is up 14.1% (chart).

### Page 5

The best is ahead. Analysts expect the sector’s earnings to grow 1.6% this
year and 22.5% next year. Pharmaceuticals' earnings are forecast to fall
5.2% this year and rise 28.6% next year (chart). Biotech is estimated to
grow earnings 46.4% next year after falling 6.2% this year.
The large caps are catching up. On September 10, the S&P 500 Health
Care index was up 6.7% ytd, against 20.6% for the S&P 600 and 17.2%
for the S&P 400. The S&P 500 sector is now up 14.1%, versus 17.8% and

17.3% (chart). The sector trades at 18.9 times forward earnings, equal to
the market multiple of 18.9. We retain our overweight rating on Health
Care.

### Page 6

(3) Consumer Staples. Two weeks ago, we argued that earnings didn't
justify Staples' valuation premium. Investors have since paid even more
for the sector. Its forward P/E has risen to 22.5 from 20.7 on October 1.
That is above both the S&P 500's 18.8 and IT's 19.1 (chart).
The multiple is doing the work again. Staples' forward P/E was down 0.3%
ytd at the end of September. Two weeks later, it is up 6.5% ytd. Forward
earnings is up 4.9% ytd and has barely moved since September (chart).

### Page 7

The priciest industry got pricier. Merchandise Retail's forward P/E rose to
33.6 from 31.0 two weeks ago, though its earnings growth is forecast to
slow to 7.1% in 2027 (chart). This is a defensive bid, not an earnings story.
We retain our underweight rating on Consumer Staples.

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】衛星產業-SpaceX D2C進展更進一步，星艦試射進展順利-20261016.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

1 
產業更新 – 衛星產業 
SpaceX D2C 進展更進一步，星艦試射進展順利 
What’s New –  SpaceX 收購新頻譜，劍指行動通訊市場 
SpaceX於 10/8宣布收購 Grain Management旗下全美 800MHz低頻頻譜，涵蓋最多 14MHz
配對頻譜，交易金額估約 80 億美元。 800MHz 具備廣域覆蓋及室內穿透優勢，可與既有
2GHz頻譜互補，改善偏遠地區通訊品質。儘管仍待 FCC核准，惟主席 Brendan Carr 對新業
者投資持正面態度，預期有利交易推進。 
Trends/Outlook – 衛星、火箭升級短期影響衛星部屬速度 
3Q26 SpaceX共完成 22次 Starlink低軌衛星部署任務，合計發射 578顆衛星，季減 16.8%，
9M26累計部署 2,167顆。單季發射數量下滑主要受任務頻率減少影響，惟單次平均部署 26.3
顆，略高於 2Q26的 25.7顆。此外，SpaceX於 9月成功透過 Starship部署 26顆新一代 V3
衛星，顯示大型運載火箭逐步具備實際衛星部署能力。另一方面，Amazon Leo於 3Q26僅完
成 1次發射任務，部署 29顆衛星，季減 81.3%，主要受運載火箭供應及發射排程限制，後續
須觀察 Vulcan、Ariane 6 及 New Glenn 等新一代火箭投入商業發射的進度。SpaceX另規劃
於路易斯安那州興建全球第四座太空港，預計 2027年動工、2029年首飛，規劃設置逾 12座
發射塔，全面建成後目標每年支援數千次 Starship飛行任務。

Investment Summary 
1. 研究部認為，EchoStar交易解決了 Starlink下一代 D2C的頻譜與容量問題，而近期收購
Grain Management 旗下全美 800MHz低頻頻譜，則進一步補強低頻地面覆蓋資源。有
了自有頻譜資源，搭配未來新一代 D2C衛星酬載之升級，SpaceX 已逐步豐富期從衛星寬
頻業者，走向整合型行動通訊服務供應商的完整資源。 
2. 維持 SpaceX 26/27 年新增 4,000/6,000 顆衛星預期不變，儘管 3Q26 新增數略低於預期，
短期受到新世代衛星、火箭轉換，影響現階段 Falcon 9星鏈任務次數部屬，惟考量下一代 
V3 衛星試射順利，4Q26有望啟動部屬，單次 Starlink 低軌衛星發射數量上限將由 29 顆
大幅上升至  60 顆，將有益於未來衛星成長在加速 ，故持續看好天上衛星供應鏈如華通
(2313 TT)、昇達科(3491 TT)、燿華(2367 TT)後市發展。

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/16

2 
SpaceX 收購新頻譜，劍指行動通訊市場 
SpaceX於 10/8宣布與數位基礎建設投資公司 Grain Management達成協議，擬收購美國全國性
800MHz低頻頻譜牌照組合，涵蓋最多 14MHz配對頻譜，市場預估交易金額約 80億美元。由於
800MHz具備較佳的訊號穿透能力及廣域覆蓋優勢，可與既有 2GHz中頻頻譜形成互補，提升室內
及偏遠地區通訊品質。該收購案目前雖仍需要 FCC進行批准，不過 FCC主席 Brendan Carr目前已
表態，認為更多投資與服務業者加入，將有助於推動新一代連線技術發展，因此預期此次收購將順
利完成，未來將加速 Starlink Mobile整合衛星與地面行動網路，強化 D2D服務覆蓋能力，並逐步
降低對傳統電信商的依賴，為 SpaceX進軍美國行動通訊市場奠定基礎。 
SpaceX於 25年進行第一次的頻譜收購，向美國衛星及電信業者 EchoStar收購旗下無線通訊頻譜
使用權及相關資產，主要獲得 AWS-4 使用權之 2GHz頻譜，初步奠定了 SpaceX在行動衛星通訊
的基礎，且逐步掌握自主頻譜資源。然衛星通訊連線存在的痛點之一，便是經常因建築物、遮蔽物
等造成訊號傳遞出現障礙，因此過去 Starlink用戶終端天線皆需要安裝在水平面 15 度以上、天空
完全無遮蔽的屋頂或陽台上。而在新取得的低頻頻譜後，SpaceX 將具備傳輸距離遠、可穿透建築
物及樹木等障礙物的優勢，有助改善衛星行動通訊的室內訊號覆蓋能力，降低對傳統基地台的依賴。
另外目前大多數行動手機均支援 800MHz頻段，因此未來消費者若使用 Starlink Mobile服務，無
須另外購買專用衛星電話。 
圖一：Starlink Mobile 服務應用示意圖                                               圖二：D2C 衛星酬載 
資料來源：SpaceX、國泰證期研究部整理 
研究部認為，EchoStar交易解決了 Starlink下一代 D2C的頻譜與容量問題，而近期的收購則進一
步補強低頻地面覆蓋資源。有了自有頻譜資源，搭配未來新一代 D2C衛星酬載之升級，SpaceX 已
逐步豐富期從衛星寬頻業者，走向整合型行動通訊服務供應商的完整資源。

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/10/16

3 
新世代衛星、火箭升級短期影響衛星部屬速度 
3Q26 SpaceX共計完成 22次 Starlink低軌衛星部署任務，合計發射 598顆衛星，包含次軌道測試
衛星 20顆，季減 11.1%，年初累計至 9M26共部署 2,167顆。其中 7、8、9月分別部署 210、
261及 107顆，9月發射數量明顯下降，推測因 Starship試飛任務導致 Falcon 9 星鏈任務減少影
響，單季任務數由 2Q26的 27次降至 22次。然 3Q26單次任務平均部署衛星顆數約為 26.3顆，
略高於 2Q26的 25.7顆，顯示整體發射數量下滑主要來自任務頻率減少，而非單次載運數量下降。
此外，SpaceX於 9月 28日成功透過 Starship Flight 14 將 26顆新一代 Starlink V3 衛星送入軌
道，為 Starship首次完成正式衛星入軌部署。隨著下一代衛星導入更大型天線與高功率通訊酬載，
單顆衛星重量及通訊容量同步提升，目前短期可能限制 Falcon 9單次部署數量，但長期隨 Starship
發射能力逐步成熟，有望加速高容量 Starlink V3衛星部署，帶動整體星系寬頻容量持續擴張。 
此外，另一位衛星運營商 Amazon Leo 3Q26僅完成 1次低軌衛星發射任務，部署 29顆衛星，數
量大幅季減 81.3%，年初至今共部署 216顆衛星。3Q26發射任務於 7/2由 ULA Atlas V 火箭執
行，其後 8、9月均無新增衛星部署，顯示 Amazon Leo 衛星部署進度仍受到運載火箭供應及發射
排程限制。我們認為，Amazon Leo目前主要瓶頸在於發射運能，而非衛星製造能力，後續須觀察
包含 ULA Vulcan、Arianespace Ariane 6及 Blue Origin New Glenn 等新一代火箭逐步投入商業
發射，是否能夠帶動 Amazon Leo發射進程提升。 
圖三：Starbase Louisiana 示意圖 
資料來源：interstellar gateway、國泰證期研究部整理 
SpaceX 於今年 8 月拍板定案，將於美國路易斯安那州南部 Vermilion Parish興建全球第四座太空
發射基地（Starbase Louisiana） ，基地位於墨西哥灣沿岸，占地約12.5萬英畝（約 5.1萬公頃） ，
面積超過紐約曼哈頓 8倍，預計 2027年正式動工，並於 2029年執行首次 Starship發射任務。基
地規劃設置超過 12座發射塔，全面建成後目標每年支援數千次 Starship飛行任務。研究部預期，
隨著 Starship逐步實現高頻率發射與火箭重複使用，新增發射基地將有助於突破既有太空港的發射
排程及運能限制，進一步加速 Starlink下一代 V3衛星部署，並提升 SpaceX大規模建置低軌衛星
的能力。

Document 487: スペインは解散総選挙へ

Source PDF: all report pdf/スペインは解散総選挙へ.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日

MURC Focus

スペインは解散総選挙へ

～右派連立政権が成立する見込み～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 解散総選挙に臨んだサンチェス首相 
スペインのペドロ・サンチェス首相が 10 月 5 日、議会を解散し、11 月 29 日に総選挙（定数 350、比例代表制）
を行うと発表した。住宅価格や家賃の高騰に対する政府の緊急政令案が、10 月 2 日に下院で否決されたことを
受けて、少数与党による政権運営の行き詰まりを意識した首相が、国民に信を問う決断をした形である 。ただし、
サンチェス首相が率いる中道左派の与党・社会労働党（PSOE）の支持率は低迷している（図表 1）。

もともと PSOE の人気は低迷しており、前回 2023 年 7 月の総選挙でも、ライバル政党である中道右派の国民
党（PP）が第一党となっていた。しかしサンチェス首相は他の左派政党や地域政党の協力を受けて組閣し、政権
を維持した経緯がある。しかし首相周辺の汚職疑惑などから PSOE の人気は回復せず、次期の総選挙では PP
と同根の右派政党である Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
図表 1. 主要政党の支持率調査 図表 2. スペインの住宅価格

（出所）各種世論調査より

ユーロ圏 スペイン
（2015年=100、季調済）
（年、四半期）
〇スペインのサンチェス首相が10月5日、議会を解散し、11月29日に総選挙を行うと発表した。次期の総選挙
では野党第一党の PP と同根の右派政党 Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
〇PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018年6月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの国内事情はもっと複雑である。 
〇仮に総選挙を経て PP と Vox の連立政権が成立しても、両派間ですり合わせるべき課題も多く、スペイン政
治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。

### Page 2

解散の直接的な理由となった住宅価格の問題であるが、住宅市況が底にあった 2015 年を基準（＝100）とす
る指数でその動きを確認すると、直近2026 年4～6 月期は201 と、ユーロ圏全体（159）を大きく上回る（図表2）。
特に 2023 年以降の上昇が目覚ましいが、堅調な景気や移民の急増を受けて住宅需要が高まったことがその主
因である。

2. 民族主義的な右派回帰とは異なる 
PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018 年 6 月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの 国内事情は複雑であるため、必ずしもその流れに合わ
ない点、留意すべきである。まず、そもそも PSOE の人気そのものが低下している。確かに景気は堅調だが、住
宅価格の急騰のような社会問題に関して、サンチェス政権は具体的な処方箋を提示できなかった。

総選挙を経て政権交代が実現し、PP が Vox と連立を組んだとしても、それを民族主義の台頭の表れと理解す
るよりも、一定の期間ごとに繰り返される左派から右派へのリバランスの流れとして理解するほうが適切だろう。 ま
たスペインの場合、右派間に対立関係があることにも注目する必要がある。地域ナショナリズムが強いスペインで
は地方分権を求める保守の力が強く、中央集権を主張する保守（つまり Vox）と対立関係にある。

中央集権を望む保守の一部に戦前・戦後のフランコ体制を理想化する向きがあることも、地域ナショナリズムと
の対立を生んでいる。地域ナショナリズム政党の中には、自らを保守と位置付けながらも、中央集権を望む保守と
の対立から、PSOE や他の左派政党（Podemos や Sumar）と歩調を合わせる動きもある。スペインの民族主義は
多様なため、同国の右派回帰を反欧州連合（EU）や反移民の文脈で捉えることはできない。

例えばスペイン南東部のカタルーニャ州では、伝統的に左派も右派も民族主義を掲げ、独立運動（あるいは高
度な自治の獲得のための運動）を展開しているが、同時に票を奪い合う関係にある。最近は左派の カタルーニャ
共和主義左翼（ERC）が劣勢である。一方、右派のカタルーニャ同盟（AC）が優勢となっており、政党支持率では
PSOE に次ぐ第 2 位となっている。

AC は右派だが独立を主張しており、中央集権を主張する Vox とは対立する。一方、カタルーニャ州でも独立
に同調しない有権者は数多く、そうした有権者は PSOE に投票する傾向が強い。カタルーニャ州はスペインでも
有数の産業地帯であるため、労働運動が盛んだったことから、伝統的に PSOE の支持が厚いためである。このよ
うに、スペイン政治はまだら模様であるため、右派回帰と民族主義を同一視することは不適切である。

### Page 3

3. 総選挙後の新政権も多難な船出となる見込み 
総選挙後にいかなる立場の新政権が成立しようとも、スペイン経済の先行きに対する懸念は残る 。確かに景気
は堅調を維持しているが、一方、冒頭で述べた住宅価格の高騰のような社会問題に対する妙薬はなかなか存在
しない。加えて、経済成長の次なる牽引役を見いだせていないという問題もある。例えばスペイン景気を牽引する
輸出だが、旅行サービスに支えられたサービスの輸出は好調でもモノの輸出は不調である（図表3）。

中南米からの移民による労働供給が潤沢とはいえ、グローバルな労働力不足やスペインの人口構成（純増で
はあるが移民流入に依存した増加）に鑑みれば、スペインでも生産性の向上は不可欠である。それに資する総固
定資本形成は、実体として建設投資に偏っており、機械投資や知的財産投資の規模は限定的である（図表 4）。
これらの投資を増やすためには国による産業政策が欠かせない。

具体的には、規制緩和や税制優遇といった措置である。これまではサンチェス首相率いる PSOE 政権が少数
与党だったということや、PSOE が中道左派であるため経済成長よりも所得分配を重視したこともあり、そうした設
備投資を後押しするような政策が必ずしも打ち出されたわけではなかった。では PP と Vox の連立政権に交代し
たことでそれが可能になるかどうかというと、その可能性も高くない。

なぜならば、PP と Vox の連立政権が強いイニシアチブの下で産業政策を“上から”推し進めようとすると、中央
集権的な色彩が強くなり、地域ナショナリズムとの間であつれきが生じる展開が予想されるためである。中央集権
志向を弱めるという現実的な判断を Vox ができるかどうかが鍵を握るが、同党にはもともと PP から右派色の濃い
グループが離脱して結党した経緯があるだけに、その余地がどの程度か定かではない。

現状では総選挙を経て PSOE から PP と Vox の連立へと政権が交代する公算が大きいが、両党ですり合わせ
るべき課題も多く、政治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。 
図表 3. GDP に占める輸出の割合 図表 4. GDP に占める固定資本形成の割合

ロシア景気概況① 景気は低迷している
ロシアの4-6月期の実質GDP（国内総生産）は前年比+1.3%と、前期（同▲0.2%）から反発した。とはいえ、7月単月の月
次GDPは同+0.6%と前月（同+1.7%）から成長が鈍化しており、ロシア景気は低迷を脱していないと判断される。一方で、
7-9月期の企業景況感指数は、鉱業と製造業が低水準で横ばいとなった。

ロシアの8月の鉱工業生産は前年比▲0.6%とマイナスに転じ、均した動きでも一進一退となった。内訳を確認すると、鉱
業が同▲1.4%と5カ月連続で前年割れとなった一方、製造業も同▲0.5%と5月以来の前年割れとなった。鉱業に関して

ロシア景気概況③ 消費は増加している

（出所）ロシア中銀（出所）ロシア中銀、ロススタット
ロシア中央銀行は9月11日に行われた金融政策決定会合で、主要政策金利（キーレート）を14.0%に据え置くことを決定
した。ロシア中銀は先行きのインフレ加速リスクを重視しているため、再び利上げに転じる可能性がある。対して、マネー
サプライ（M2）の増勢は利下げにもかかわらず弱い。

### Page 8

7 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ロシア景気概況⑦ 金利は上昇、株価は下落後に横ばい
ロシアの7-9月期の長期金利は上昇した。中銀による金融緩和が進んだにもかかわらず、インフレ圧力の高まりや歳出
の膨張が嫌気されたものと考えられる。一方、同期のロシアの株価は下落後に横ばい。金融緩和が進んだものの、積極
的な買い材料に乏しいものと考えられる。
10年国債流通利回り 株価（RTS指数）
（出所）モスクワ取引所（出所）ロシア財務省

### Page 9

8 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
ロシア景気概況⑧ 通貨は対ドル・対円で下落
ルーブル相場 国際収支
（出所）ロシア中銀（出所）ロシア中銀
ロシアの通貨ルーブルの7-9月期の相場は、対ドル・対円とも下落した。ロシア中銀による利下げを受けて国内のルーブ
ル需給が緩み、ルーブル安が進んだものと考えられる。一方、国際収支を確認すると、ルーブル買いの主因となるはず
の貿易黒字（財収支黒字）は、歴史的な原油高にもかかわらず拡大が限定的なままである。

### Page 10

9 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
トルコ景気概況① 景気は減速している
トルコの4-6月期の実質GDPは前年比+2.3%と、4四半期連続で伸びが鈍化した。内需が景気を下支えした反面、軟調な
輸出が経済の成長を下押しした。続く7-9月期の企業景況感指数は、製造業・サービス業ともに中立水準を上回ったもの
の、改善は足踏みした。イラン情勢が企業の景況感の重しになっていると考えられる。

事件  1
10/20 SpaceX 第三季營收 145.8 億美元、⾸度單季獲利，並與特斯拉各設特別委
員會研議合併
五⼤重點
1  SpaceX 公布第三季營收 145.8 億美元，年增 161% ，⾼於市場預期約 128.7 億美元；淨利 44 億美

元，為 6 ⽉上市後⾸度單季獲利，去年同期虧損 11 億美元。
2   ⼈⼯智慧部⾨（ SI ）營收 75.6 億美元，較第⼆季 26 億美元增近兩倍，⾸度超過星鏈連線業務的
52 億美元； SI 部⾨營業利益 4.31 億美元，第⼆季為虧損 13 億美元。

### Page 2

3   財務⻑說明， SI 營收成⻑主要來⾃雲端算⼒服務合約放量；⾺斯克表⽰算⼒出租採近似現貨價
格⽽⾮⻑約，⽬標內部使⽤與對外出租各半。
4   第三季資本⽀出 195.9 億美元，其中 164.4 億美元投⼊ AI ；公司預估第四季資本⽀出 350 億⾄ 400
億美元，並預估 2027 年第⼀季營收 330 億⾄ 370 億美元。
5   ⾺斯克證實 SpaceX 與特斯拉各已設⽴獨⽴董事特別委員會研議合併，但雙⽅尚未就條件達成
協議；財報後 SpaceX 股價盤後⼀度漲 5.5% 。
三⼤結論
1  SpaceX 把 AI 算⼒租賃轉成營收與營業利益，屬於 AI 投資可回收的正⾯證據，有利於台灣伺服
器、散熱與電⼒設備的訂單能⾒度；但第四季資本⽀出上看 400 億美元，現⾦流能否跟上仍待驗
證。
2   算⼒以接近現貨價出租，收⼊會隨 GPU 租賃價格波動；當 GPU 租賃價格下跌或客戶延後資本
⽀出時，應降低依賴外部融資的雲端與算⼒租賃部位，保留具定價權與現⾦流的上游核⼼。
3   特斯拉合併案尚無條件，屬尚未成⽴的交易；投資上應以 630 億美元在⼿訂單轉成營收與⾃由

現⾦流的速度評估，⽽⾮以合併題材或年度經常性收⼊⽬標提⾼估值。
事件  2
10/20 Anthropic 上市招股說明書草案：上半年營收 163 億美元，算⼒承諾逾
6,000 億美元
五⼤重點
1  Anthropic 的 IPO 招股說明書（ S-1 ）草案顯⽰， 2026 年上半年營收 163 億美元，約為去年同期
11 億美元的 15 倍（推算）；上市估值可能達 2 兆美元。
2   上半年淨虧損 807 億美元，其中約 720 億美元來⾃可轉換票據與融資⼯具的公允價值變動，屬
⾮現⾦會計調整；上半年算⼒與基礎設施⽀出近 90 億美元，已超過 2025 年全年 73 億美元。
3   未來約⼗年已簽訂約 4,500 億美元不可取消雲端算⼒合約，包括 Google Cloud 1,110 億、 AWS
1,100 億與微軟 310 億美元；連同晶⽚租賃等義務，已確定的基建承諾逾 6,000 億美元。
4   草案揭露上半年約 20% 營收來⾃兩家客戶，並預期少數客戶仍將貢獻顯著⽐重；公司另列出算

⼒短缺、依賴少數基建夥伴與重⼤ AI 安全事件等⾵險。

### Page 3

5   公司表⽰需要的算⼒多於⼤型雲端業者能提供的量，正洽談第三⽅資料中⼼開發，未來數年算
⼒⽀出最⾼可能達 7,500 億美元。
三⼤結論
1   模型公司營收快速放⼤，⽀持 AI 推論⽤量開始轉成服務收⼊的判斷； 4,500 億美元雲端合約也
提⾼⼤型雲端業者在⼿訂單能⾒度，有利先進製程、記憶體與資料中⼼供應鏈。
2   營收規模仍遠低於算⼒承諾，且客戶集中， AI 投資回收仍須以營收、⽑利與⾃由現⾦流逐季驗
證；當模型價格競爭侵蝕收⼊或上市延宕時，應先降低⾼槓桿雲端與算⼒租賃曝險。
3   ⼤型 AI 公司上市將與既有 AI 股票競逐資⾦，短期可能加劇類股波動；配置上維持⾼品質 AI 供應
鏈核⼼並分批承接，不以單⼀上市事件追⾼。
事件  3
10/20 台灣 9 ⽉外銷訂單 1,142.1 億美元創新⾼，年增 62.4% 優於預期
五⼤重點
1   經濟部統計處公布， 9 ⽉外銷訂單 1,142.1 億美元，年增 62.4% ，連續 20 個⽉正成⻑，並連續兩
個⽉突破千億美元、創歷年單⽉新⾼；市場預期年增約 58.7% 。

2   資通訊產品年增 87.3% 、電⼦產品年增 71% ，增速較 8 ⽉的 100.5% 與 83.9% 放緩；光學器材年
減 5.6% ，統計處說明主因⾯板需求保守、客戶拉貨放緩。
3   統計處說明， AI 、⾼效能運算與雲端需求撐住訂單，優於預期部分主要來⾃⼿機、筆電等消費
性電⼦新品拉貨，但無法判斷新品訂單是由數量或價格帶動，國際原物料漲價也墊⾼⾦額。
4   前三季累計 8,192 億美元，年增 56% ，已超過 2025 年全年；統計處預估 10 ⽉接單 1,125 億⾄
1,159 億美元、年增 62% ⾄ 67% ，全年突破 1.1 兆美元機率⾼。
5  9 ⽉實績⾼於發布前研究估計的 1,060 億⾄ 1,091 億美元上緣，統計處也坦⾔上⽉預估時對消費
新品組合較為保守。
三⼤結論
1   外銷訂單與 9 ⽉出⼝年增 60.9% 、台積電上修全年展望相互印證， AI 供應鏈需求仍強，台股維
持先進製程、伺服器與⾼階零組件的核⼼配置。
2   優於預期部分來⾃消費新品與原物料價格，訂單成⻑並⾮全⾯擴散；當外銷訂單、 PMI 新訂單

與營收連續兩期轉弱時，應先減少 ODM 與⼀般零組件等⾼ beta 部位。

### Page 4

3   出⼝美元收⼊⽀撐新台幣，但外資賣超與美元利率仍構成反向⼒量；以台幣計價的投資⼈應分
散美元與台幣曝險，並保留部分美元短券作為匯率緩衝。
事件  4
10/20 美國 9 ⽉新屋開⼯⽉減 5.2% ⾄年率 122.1 萬戶，遠低於預期
五⼤重點
1   美國商務部普查局公布， 9 ⽉新屋開⼯季調年率 122.1 萬戶，⽉減 5.2% ，遠低於市場預期 130.5
萬戶； 8 ⽉由 127.5 萬上修⾄ 128.8 萬，年減 7.4% 。
2   單戶住宅開⼯ 85.9 萬戶，⽉減 4.6% ；營建許可 140 萬戶，⽉減 0.2% 、略⾼於預期 139.5 萬，但
單戶許可降⾄ 84.5 萬戶、⽉減 4% ，未來單戶開⼯動能偏弱。
3   同⽇全美不動產協會公布 9 ⽉成屋待售指數⽉減 7.3% ，降⾄有紀錄以來最低，遠差於預期的減
0.5% ，四⼤區域全數下滑，顯⽰房市疲弱已由開⼯延伸到買⽅成交。
4   數據公布後美國 10 年期公債殖利率下跌約 2 ⾄ 5 個基點⾄ 5.09% ⾄ 5.12% ， 30 年期跌 5 個基點⾄

5.426% ， 2 年期微升⾄ 4.787% ，曲線趨平。
5   聯準會將於 10/27 ⾄ 28 開會， CME FedWatch 顯⽰ 10 ⽉維持利率不變機率 83.9% ， 12 ⽉累計升
息 1 碼機率 76.1% ，房市⾛弱尚未改變年底升息定價。
三⼤結論
1   新屋開⼯與成屋待售同步下滑，顯⽰⾼利率已由住宅信⼼傳導到實際建案與成交，住宅投資成
為美國擴張轉趨緩的早期證據；但就業與消費尚未同步轉弱，仍屬利率敏感部⾨降溫。
2   當⾮農就業、職位空缺與零售銷售也連續轉弱、衰退⾵險超過通膨⾵險時，應把短券到期資⾦
逐步轉向⻑天期公債；在此之前，⻑債仍以分段評估為主，短券承接政策不確定性。
3   營建與房地產相關股票承受⾼利率與成本壓⼒，美股配置宜以⾃由現⾦流與資產負債表穩健的
⼤型公司為核⼼，不以單⼀房市數據預期聯準會轉向降息。
事件  5
10/20 美超微上修第⼀財季營收⾄ 185 億⾄ 195 億美元，⾼於原財測上緣
五⼤重點

### Page 5

1   美超微（ Supermicro ）公布 2027 財年第⼀季（截⾄ 9/30 ）初步營運，營收預估 185 億⾄ 195 億
美元，明顯⾼於原財測 145 億⾄ 155 億美元，公司歸因於 AI 平台需求增加。
2   ⽑利率預估 12.0% ⾄ 12.5% ，⾼於原財測 10.4% ⾄ 10.8% ，公司說明主因客戶與產品組合改
善；數字為未經會計師核閱的初估值。
3   美超微股價盤後⼀度漲逾 13% ；正式財報與法說會訂於美東時間 11/3 舉⾏。
4   營收上修幅度以區間中值計算約 27% （推算），與同⽇ SpaceX 算⼒租賃收⼊放⼤、台灣 9 ⽉資
通訊外銷訂單年增 87.3% ⼀致，顯⽰ AI 伺服器出貨仍在加速。
5   即使上修後，美超微⽑利率仍約 12% ，遠低於上游晶⽚與記憶體廠，反映伺服器組裝環節競爭
激烈、議價⼒有限。
三⼤結論
1  AI 伺服器出貨上修⽀持台灣 ODM 、散熱與 PCB 的訂單能⾒度，但組裝環節⽑利率偏低，營收
成⻑未必轉成同等獲利；配置上先進封裝、記憶體優於⼀般組裝。

2   伺服器廠營收快速放⼤時，存貨與應收帳款通常先上升；當應收、存貨週轉或客戶信⽤惡化
時，應讓 ODM 部位低於上游核⼼配置。
3   美超微客戶包含新雲端業者，若 CoreWeave 等⾼槓桿客戶融資條件惡化，訂單與回款可能受
影響；投資上應以 11/3 正式財報的現⾦流與存貨數據確認，再決定是否提⾼部位。
事件  6
10/20 韓股 KOSPI 重挫 3.73% 創半年新低，資料中⼼股 Elice 上市⾸⽇跌 32%
五⼤重點
1   韓國綜合股價指數 KOSPI 10/20 下跌 215.27 點、跌 3.73% ，收 5,552.74 點，為 4 ⽉ 7 ⽇以來最低
收盤；盤中⼀度跌 4.76% 、跌破 5,500 點。
2   三星電⼦跌 2.98% 、 SK 海⼒⼠跌 4.22% ；外資盤中賣超約 6,367 億韓元，機構賣超約 4,670 億韓
元，散戶與⼀般法⼈承接。
3  AI 資料中⼼基建公司 Elice 於科斯達克上市，募資 2,010 億韓元為該市場今年最⼤ IPO ，散戶認

購約 400 倍，但⾸⽇收盤較承銷價跌 32% ，為韓國⼤型 IPO 史上最差⾸⽇表現。
4   同⽇⽇經 225 指數跌 1.3% 收 64,075 點、創⼀個⽉新低，台股加權指數跌 1.1% 收 46,293 點、為 9
⽉ 17 ⽇以來最低，亞洲科技股同步承壓。

### Page 6

5  KOSPI 在 10/9 ⾄ 10/16 ⼀週已重挫 14.46% ， 10/20 在連續兩個交易⽇反彈後再度⼤跌，顯⽰記
憶體與 AI 股的反彈缺乏延續性。
三⼤結論
1  Elice 上市失利與 KOSPI 重挫，反映市場對 AI 資料中⼼融資與獲利的疑慮由美國新雲端擴散到
亞洲，記憶體與科技股對全球⾵險偏好⾼度敏感。
2   當記憶體報價、出⼝與外資流向三者同向改善時，才應分批回補韓股；在此之前，應控制槓桿
與單⼀記憶體集中度，持有韓股時保留部分美元避險。
3   台股與韓股同步回檔，但台灣外銷訂單與出⼝仍強，屬基本⾯強與市場回檔並存；當領漲股跌
破趨勢且信⽤利差擴⼤時，應先削減⾼ beta 與槓桿部位。
事件  7
10/20 軟銀集團 5 年期 CDS 升⾄ 432 個基點，創 2022 年 11 ⽉以來新⾼
五⼤重點
1   軟銀集團 5 年期信⽤違約交換（ CDS ） 10/20 報 432 個基點，為 2022 年 11 ⽉初以來最⾼；⼀週前
突破 400 個基點後再擴⼤逾 30 個基點，為⽇本主要企業中最⾼，較第⼆名的⽇產汽⾞⾼逾 130 個

基點。
2   信⽤壓⼒來⾃市場擔憂舉債⽀撐的 AI 投資， CoreWeave 財務壓⼒使市場聚焦 AI 產業借貸；融資
成本上升，以及軟銀評估收購保全公司 Allied Universal ，也加重資⾦需求。
3   軟銀股價較 6 ⽉⾼點下跌約 50% ； 2022 年 CDS 處於類似⽔準時，願景基⾦認列重⼤虧損，集團
轉為防守。
4   有分析估計軟銀⾄ 2027 年 3 ⽉的年度現⾦流出⾄少 60 億美元，若推進收購需另籌資⾦，貸款價
值⽐（ LTV ）可能逼近公司⾃設的 25% 上限。
5   同期 CoreWeave 五年期 CDS ⼀度逾 1,100 個基點，美國 CCC 級⾼收益債利差突破 12% ，但投資
級公司債利差仍約 0.8% ，信⽤壓⼒集中在⾼槓桿 AI 投資者。
三⼤結論
1   軟銀信⽤利差擴⼤屬於 AI 融資鏈中⾼槓桿投資者的壓⼒，與⼤型雲端業者的投資級信⽤仍有區
隔，不宜外推為整個 AI 建置週期的信⽤斷裂。

### Page 7

2   當⼤型雲端業者在⼿訂單轉營收放慢、投資級利差同步擴⼤或設備供應商應收週轉惡化，三項
中出現兩項時，應降低雲端、⾼收益債與⾼營運資⾦供應鏈部位。
3   ⽇股應挑選具定價權、海外競爭⼒的⿓頭，降低依賴融資的控股與 AI 投資型標的；⾼收益債避
開 CCC 與⾼資本⽀出新雲端，待利差回落再加碼。
事件  8
10/20 通⽤汽⾞第三季調整後 EPS 3.70 美元，原物料成本上升使股價跌 6.83%
五⼤重點
1   通⽤汽⾞公布第三季營收 500 億美元，年增 2.9% ，調整後息稅前利益 41 億美元、年增 21.6% ；
調整後 EPS 3.70 美元，年增 32.2% ，僅略⾼於市場預期 3.69 美元。
2   全年調整後息稅前利益財測由 140 億⾄ 160 億收斂為 145 億⾄ 155 億美元，調整後 EPS 財測收斂
為 12.75 ⾄ 13.75 美元；汽⾞⾃由現⾦流財測上修⾄ 115 億⾄ 125 億美元，並宣布 200 億美元庫藏股
授權。
3   財務⻑說明，熱軋鋼與銅現貨價⾃ 7 ⽉以來上漲 10% ⾄ 15% ，約⽐去年均價⾼ 50% ，今年將增

加約 4 億美元成本、多數落在第四季；連同物流與 DRAM ，全年成本逆⾵約 20 億美元。
4   今年以來關稅成本約 18 億美元，全年關稅總成本預估落在 25 億⾄ 35 億美元區間低端；北美調
整後息稅前利益率 8.7% ，國際業務利益年減 87.7% 。
5   通⽤汽⾞股價 10/20 收跌 6.83% ，為 2025 年 7 ⽉以來最⼤單⽇跌幅；同⽇道瓊指數漲 1.02% ，標
普 500 指數漲 0.29% 。
三⼤結論
1   獲利⼩幅優於預期仍遭賣壓，顯⽰市場聚焦原物料與記憶體成本上升對第四季及 2027 年利潤
的侵蝕，製造業成本轉嫁能⼒成為財報季評價重點。
2   銅與鋼價上漲同時反映電網、資料中⼼與製造需求，對上游⾦屬有⽀撐但壓縮下游⽑利；銅應
等待電網投資、庫存與新訂單共同確認，再分批提⾼⻑線配置。
3   當原物料成本持續上升⽽企業無法轉嫁時，應降低⼀般製造與消費耐久財部位，偏好具定價權
與⾃由現⾦流的公司；通⽤汽⾞上修現⾦流並擴⼤庫藏股，屬資本回饋⽽⾮成⻑加速。

事件  9
10/19 美國國⼟安全部禁⽌聯邦機構採購並移除 DeepSeek 產品

### Page 8

五⼤重點
1   美國國⼟安全部 10/19 依《聯邦採購供應鏈安全法》發布排除與移除命令，禁⽌聯邦⺠事機構
採購杭州深度求索（ DeepSeek ）及其關聯企業的資訊產品與服務。
2   命令要求從聯邦⺠事機構系統移除相關產品，範圍延伸⾄以 DeepSeek 技術建構的第三⽅產品
與服務；國⼟安全部說明係依聯邦採購安全委員會建議與相關證據作成。
3   中國商務部 10/20 表⽰強烈不滿、堅決反對，指美⽅泛化國家安全、將科技經貿問題政治化，
敦促美⽅撤銷措施，並呼籲⽤好中美⼈⼯智慧對話機制。
4   中國外交部發⾔⼈⽑寧 10/20 表⽰，中⽅⼀貫反對泛化國家安全概念，將堅決維護中國企業正
當權益；商務部同⽇另就美國與以⾊列以竊取商業機密為由制裁中企表⽰將採取必要措施。
5  DeepSeek 於 10/17 發布 V4.1 Flash 模型，在第三⽅ Agent Arena 開源模型榜位列第⼀、 API 價格
不變，顯⽰中國開放權重模型仍具成本競爭⼒。
三⼤結論
1   美⽅把限制範圍由晶⽚出⼝擴⼤到 AI 模型與軟體採購，顯⽰中美科技競爭延伸到應⽤層；中美

貿易休戰僅延⾄ 2027 年 1 ⽉ 10 ⽇，稀⼟許可與 H200 交貨仍有履約⾵險，供應鏈不確定性並未消
除。
2   當中⽅以稀⼟、出⼝管制或對美企調查作為反制時，應降低⾼度依賴中國供應與中國市場的科
技部位；在此之前，中國資產維持選擇性配置，以具出⼝競爭⼒的新產業為主。
3   中國開放權重模型的使⽤量⾼，但使⽤量不等於收⼊，美國限制將使其在政府與企業市場的變
現更受阻；軟體股仍應以客戶留存、在⼿訂單與⾃由現⾦流驗證 AI 變現。
事件  10
10/20 德國 10 ⽉ ZEW 景氣預期指數降⾄ 28.1 ，歐元區降⾄四個⽉低點
五⼤重點
1   歐洲經濟研究中⼼（ ZEW ）公布，德國 10 ⽉景氣預期指數由 34.7 降⾄ 28.1 ，低於市場預期約
30 ；現況指數則由負 47.1 ⼤幅改善⾄負 35.9 ，仍深陷負值。
2   歐元區 10 ⽉景氣預期指數由 25.8 降⾄ 18.5 ，為四個⽉低點，低於預期約 23.6 ；歐元區現況指數
由負 13.9 ⼩降⾄負 14.8 。

### Page 9

3  ZEW 總裁 Wambach 說明，德國經濟正逐步⾛出停滯，但復甦並⾮⾃我持續，⾼能源價格壓縮
家庭購買⼒、推⾼⽣產成本並使通膨居⾼。
4   產業別中，銀⾏與保險預期分別⼤降 18.4 與 9.0 點，服務與電信轉弱，鋼鐵、⾦屬與電⼦則改
善，反映科技與製造訂單仍有⽀撐。
5   調查顯⽰歐元區 67.1% 受訪分析師預期未來數⽉經濟活動持平， 25.7% 預期改善、 7.2% 預期惡
化， ZEW 指伊朗衝突難解的不確定性壓抑投資⼈信⼼。
三⼤結論
1   德國現況改善但預期轉弱，代表歐洲製造業底部回升仍受能源成本牽制，成⻑廣度不及美國與
亞洲 AI 供應鏈，歐股不宜因景氣數據回升⽽全⾯加碼週期股。
2   能源推升通膨使歐洲央⾏難以迅速轉向寬鬆；當歐元區調和物價指數連續回落且能源價格穩定
時，才應提⾼歐洲週期股⽐重，在此之前以低負債、⾼現⾦流與國防基建受益者為主。
3   法國財政⾵險仍是歐元資產的主要分化來源，歐元部位應控制集中度；當 10/23 評級遭下調或
法德利差持續⾼於 150 個基點時，應進⼀步減少法國主權與銀⾏股曝險。

待確認

期 15 倍以 163 億除以 11 億美元推算。
2. 單⼀來源或未公開⽂件：事件 2 數據來⾃媒體所⾒之 Anthropic 招股說明書草案，公司尚未公開遞件、亦未評論，最
終數字以正式 S-1 為準；事件 7 的 2027 年 3 ⽉前現⾦流出 60 億美元與 LTV 估計為單⼀分析師估算；事件 6 外資與機構賣
超⾦額為盤中統計， Elice ⾸⽇跌幅另有 28% 的盤中報價。
3. 預期值差異：事件 10 德國 ZEW 市場預期各家調查介於 30 ⾄ 31.6 、歐元區介於 23.6 ⾄ 24.2 ；事件 4 美國 10 年期殖利率
收盤各報價介於 5.09% ⾄ 5.12% 。
4. 官⽅尚未公布：美超微正式財報待 11/3 公布； Anthropic 上市時程與估值尚未確定； SpaceX 與特斯拉合併條件尚未

sherman.shang@fubon.com huangyuan.yang@fubon.com

聯發科（2454 TT）

ASIC 轉折即將到來；EMIB 強化 2028 年供應案例

重申買進，維持目標價 6,000 元，潛在漲幅 34% 
1. 客製化 ASIC 營收展望：更高內容價值推動下一成長階段 
2. 我們對 Intel EMIB 供應的看法：量化 2028 年供應路徑 
3. 獲利預覽，修正：3Q26 表現更強，2027 年度獲利展望維持

客製化 ASIC 營收展望：更高內容價值推動下一成長階段 
我們的聯發科客製化 ASIC 模型預測 2026/27/28 年度營收分別為 22 億美元、165 億
美元、585 億美元，毛利率為 38-39%。2027 年，350 萬個 v8 封裝以每個 4,500 美
元產生 157.2 億美元營收，年底時 V9 早期營收非常有限。2028 年，360 萬個 v9 封
裝以每個 15,000 美元產生 540 億美元營收，並由 v8 營收補充 25.2 億美元。此外，
我們認為 SpaceX 可能在 2028 年底提供額外上行空間，但尚未確認。更高的 v9 內
容價值，而非僅僅是出貨量，推動轉折點。平均售價包含部分運算供應、I/O 及封裝
/整合。Google 內部需求支持此論點，即使未獲 Anthropic 訂單。根據我們的分配預
測，2027 年分配給聯發科 354 萬顆，佔 Google 內部需求的 60%。TPU V10 目前市
場充斥許多雜音與傳聞。依我們看法，討論此項目為時過早，因其屬於 2029 年底
或 2030 年的計畫，但這也顯示市場對聯發科客製化 ASIC 營收展望期望極高。我們
認為這並不健康，任何雜音或傳聞都可能造成短期波動。然而，我們仍維持對聯發
科客製化 ASIC 業務中長期正面看法。

我們對 Intel EMIB 供應的看法：量化 2028 年供應路徑 
我們的 EMIB 試算表假設 2026/27/28 年底每月可用產能分別為 2 萬/4 萬/6 萬，2028
年平均產能為 5 萬。以 70% v9 分配、100%利用率、每個 EMIB 有 16 個封裝位置、
80%組裝良率計算，2028 年模型產出達 540 萬個良品封裝，足以覆蓋我們預測的
360 萬個 v9，並有 1.5 倍餘裕。若利用率降至 80%、良率降至 70%，產出降至 380
萬，僅剩 5.5%緩衝。分配並非合約權利；基板、邏輯/HBM 供應及認證仍然關鍵。
除了 TPU 外，我們調查顯示 AWS 目前考慮採用 EMIB，使 Intel EMIB 供應在 2028 年
更具前景。鑑於目前產能良率仍低，我們認為Intel 策略是更積極擴建產能，包括資
助其基板合作夥伴如欣興、Ibiden 等。

獲利預覽，修正：3Q26 表現更強，2027 年度獲利展望維持 
我們維持買進評等 ， 目標股價不變為6,000 元 ， 隨著市場焦點轉向客製化ASIC 專案
量產。聯發科 3Q26 營收達 1680.6 億元（季增 10.4%、年增 18.3%） ，優於我們先前
預估的 1525.7 億元；營業利潤/稅後淨利分別增加約 10.2%至 264 億元/284.1 億元。
每股稅後盈餘由 16.19 元提升至 17.71 元 ， 較市場共識高出15.8%， 主要受營收與費用
吸收帶動。4Q26 我們維持營收預估 2021.7 億元及稅後淨利預估 364.2 億元。預期
EPS 將季增 28.2%至 22.71 元，儘管毛利率降至 45.0%，仍與市場共識大致相符。展
望 2027 年，除 TPU 外，旗艦 5G SoC、車用及 Wi-Fi 7 前景依然強勁，將強化聯發科
進入 2027 年的成長動能。整體而言，我們對聯發科維持正向看法，認為短期回檔受
市場雜音影響，反而是另一個進場時機。

Fubon's Rating System 
Rating Definition 
Buy Expected absolute return to be over 15% within the next 6 months  
Neutral  Expected absolute return to be level within the next 6 months 
Sell  Expected absolute return to be over negative 15% within the next 6 months  
Not Rated (NR)  Pursuant to Fubon acting in deals involving the company or there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an investment rating  
Under Review Fubon is in the process of determining an investment rating and will assign a rating within the 3 - to 6-month horizon

Sector Rating Definition 
Overweight Sector expected to outperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months 
Market Weight  Sector expected to perform in line with the broader market averages within the next 6-12 months 
Underweight  Sector expected to underperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months.

Document 649: 报道：德国10月达成协议以来首次启动招标，出售战略储备中的柴油、取暖油和原油

Source PDF: all report pdf/报道：德国10月达成协议以来首次启动招标，出售战略储备中的柴油、取暖油和原油.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

德国同⽇推出两项能源举措：⽯油⽅⾯，储备机构 EBV 启动招标，出售柴油、取暖油及原
油，落实 G7 协调放储协议，德国承诺释放最多 1500 万桶；天然⽓⽅⾯，运营商 THE 斥资约
2.45 亿欧元签订额外供⽓合同，以应对当前储⽓率仅 61.1% 、较去年同期低逾 14 个百分点
的库存危机。
摘要：
德国正从战略储备两端同步发⼒：既向市场释放⽯油，⼜斥资锁定冬季天然⽓供应，折射出欧
洲最⼤经济体在能源安全层⾯持续承压的现实。
10 ⽉ 23 ⽇周五，德国⽯油储备机构 EBV 发出招标邀请，拟出售战略储备中的柴油、取暖油及原
油。据路透社，这是德国⾃ G7 国家 10 ⽉初达成协调放储协议以来的⾸次实际执⾏。
与此同时，德国天然⽓市场运营商 Trading Hub Europe （ THE ）披露，为保障冬季供应安全，
德国已斥资近 2.45 亿欧元（约 2.74 亿美元）签订额外供⽓合同，背景是当前德国天然⽓库存⽔平
较去年同期⼤幅偏低。
两项举措同⽇公布，显⽰德国在油⽓领域均⾯临供应侧压⼒，市场关注的焦点将集中于此次⽯

油招标的最终成交量，以及天然⽓库存能否在⼊冬前得到有效补充。
⽯油放储： G7 协议落地，德国率先执⾏
据路透社， EBV 于周五向相关企业分别发出购买邀请，涉及产品涵盖柴油、取暖油及原油。由
于各企业收到的邀约数量不同，此次招标的总规模暂⽆法得到确认， EBV 亦未在正常⼯作时间
内回应路透社的置评请求。
此次放储是 G7 国家 10 ⽉初协调⾏动的组成部分。彼时，在美国总统特朗普的压⼒下，柴油价格
⾼企已成为 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇美国中期选举的重要政治议题， G7 国家同意从紧急储备中释放共计 1 亿桶⽯
油。
该协议由国际能源署（ IEA ）统筹协调，其中包括优先释放 2000 万桶柴油，以及 3 ⽉伊朗战争爆
发时承诺释放但尚未动⽤的剩余储量。
德国经济部⻓ Katherina Reiche 本⽉早些时候表⽰，德国承诺释放⾄多 1500 万桶柴油、取暖油
及原油，是欧洲国家中出资规模最⼤的，但未披露具体时间表。
德国联邦参议院上周已为放储扫清⽴法障碍，将法定最低储备要求从 90 天消费量临时下调⾄ 82

天。
从市场影响来看，欧洲基准柴油期货较原油期货的溢价 9 ⽉底曾触及每桶 95 美元的历史⾼位，截
⾄周五已回落⾄约 76 美元。此次德国向市场注⼊额外柴油与取暖油，预计将对西北欧裂解价差
及内陆产品升⽔形成压制，原油部分的市场影响相对有限。
天然⽓储备：库存告急，专项合同护航冬季
天然⽓⽅⾯，形势同样不容乐观。 THE 数据显⽰，截⾄ 10 ⽉ 21 ⽇，德国天然⽓储存设施的充填
率仅为 61.1% ，远低于去年同期的 75.4% 。

Document 660: 時事雑感(2026年11月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/時事雑感(2026年11月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

注目のキーワード 「金融レジリエンス」
資産形成というと、 NISAや株式投資などで 「お金を増やす」 ことに目が向きがちです。 一方、 物価上昇や失業、 病気、 災害
など、 家計には予期せぬ出来事が生じます。 特にたくわえが十分でない世帯では、 長期的な投資に先立ち、 急な出費や収
入減に対応できる生活防衛資金の確保も重要です。 こう した備えは、 投資した資産を不利な時期に取り崩すことを避け、 長
期的な資産形成を続けるための支えにもなります。 したがって資産形成には、 「どれだけ資産を増やせるか」 だけでなく、
「家計が予期せぬシ ョ ッ クにどれだけ耐え、 そこから立て直せるか」 という視点も欠かせません。 家計がシ ョ ッ クに耐える力
の重要性は、 近年のコロナ禍や物価上昇を経て、 改めて認識されています。
こう した視点に関わる概念の一つが 「金融レジリエンス」 です。 金融レジリエンスとは、 経済的なシ ョ ッ クに直面しても生
活を維持し、 立て直す力を指します。 OECD/INFEの国際調査では、 「主な収入源を失った場合に、 借入れや転居をせずに

生活費をどのく らいの期間まかなえるか」 「月収に相当する大きな支出を、 借入れや家族 ・ 友人からの援助なしに支払える
か」 といった設問で測定しています。
もっ とも、 生活を立て直すために必要なのは、 金融面での備えや対処力だけではありません。 困難な状況を受け止め、 適
応し、 立ち直っていく心理的な力、 すなわち 「心理的レジリエンス」 も重要です。
また、 どれだけ備え、 計画を立てていても、 将来には予測できない部分が残ります。 収入や物価、 金利、 投資収益、 家族状
況は不確実で、 資産形成に絶対安心の 「唯一の正解」 はありません。 そこで参考になるのが 「ネガティブ ・ ケイパビリティ」 で
す。 これは、 すぐには答えの出ない状況や、 不確実性、 曖昧さに直面しても、 性急に結論を求めず、 その状況に耐えながら考
え続ける力を意味します。
経済的なシ ョ ッ クに備え、 対処し、 生活を立て直す 「金融レジリエンス」 。 困難な状況に心理的に適応し、 回復する 「心理的

レジリエンス」 。 そして、 不確実性と付き合いながら考え続ける 「ネガティブ ・ ケイパビリティ」 。 これら三つの力をあわせて育
むことが、 不確実性の高い時代における資産形成を支える重要な視点ではないでし ょ うか。
（資産運用調査部　研究員　小林 菜）
時の政府にと っては鬼門とされる米国の中間選挙が11月3日に行われる。 1934年以降の23回の中間選挙を
振り返ると、 現職大統領の政党が上下両院とも議席を増やしたのは1934年のF.ルーズベル ト大統領と、 2002年
のG.W.ブッシュ大統領の2回しかない。 ルーズベル ト大統領は大恐慌下のニューディール政策、 ブッシュ大統領は
9.11同時テロへの対応がそれぞれ支持された。 因みに、 その23回の選挙のうち、 下院の議席数を増やしたのは前
述の2名のほかには、 1998年のクリン トン大統領だけである （同選挙では上院の議席数は変わらなかった） 。 一方、
上院については前述の2名の他に、 1962年のケネディ、 1970年のニクソン、 2018年の トランプ、 2022年のバイ

デン各大統領が議席を増やしている。 下院に比べ、 上院で政権が議席を増やしているケースが多い背景には、 1回
の選挙における上院の改選議席数は全体の3分の1であることが影響している。
さて、 こう した過去のケースを振り返ると、 中間選挙では余程の実績や追い風が無いと、 下院議席数を減らす結
果となる厳しい 「中間テス ト」 ということがわかる。 現 トランプ政権は物価高やイラン問題で国内の批判も高いこと
から、 過去の例に倣えば下院共和党の議席数は減少する可能性が高い。 現在の議席数は、 下院 （全435議席） では
共和党は218、 民主党は214議席 （無所属共和党員1、 空席2） と、 両党の議席数は拮抗し、 逆転の可能性は十分に
ある。 これに対し上院 （全100議席） では、 共和党は53議席を有しているが、 そのうち22議席が改選対象と、 守るべ
き議席数が多い。
米国の各種世論調査では、 下院のみならず、 上院でも民主党が過半数を奪還するとの見方もある。 上下両院で
民主党が過半数を握る事態となれば、 向こう2年間の トランプ政権は予算案を中心に厳しい政策運営を迫られ、 債

券市場や株式市場への悪影響も危惧される。   （嶌峰 義清）
時事雑感

### Page 3

欧州
(株)日本総合研究所 欧州経済展望 2026年10月
◆財部門が生産活動の重石に
足元のユーロ圏景気は 、サービス主導で底堅く推
移。７月のサービス業生産は増勢を維持 。一方、
鉱工業や建設業など財部門の生産活動は低調。
先行きも、生産活動の二極化は続く見通し。旺盛
なＡＩ需要を背景に 、情報通信を中心にサービス
業は好調を維持するものの、エネルギーコストの高止
まりや対中競争力の低下を受けて 、中間財や消費
財の生産は低迷が長引く公算大 。加えて、金利高
による需要減少が、建設活動を抑制する見込み。
◆物価や金利の上昇が内需を下押し
先行きの景気は、インフレ加速や金利上昇を受け
て減速する見通し 。まず、物価高が個人消費を下
押しする公算 。９月の消費者物価は前年比＋
3.8％と、前月から伸びが拡大。資源高が続くことで、
来年初にかけてインフレ率がさらに上昇すると予想。
加えて、金利上昇が住宅投資や設備投資を抑制
する見込み。ＥＣＢは、９月の理事会で政策金利
を0.25％ポイント引き上げ 。インフレ加速が見込ま

れるなか、年内にも追加利上げを行う見通し。
政治面からも金利上昇圧力が増大 。フランスでは、
来年度予算案で財政赤字の対ＧＤＰ比がＥＵ
目標を超えることが示され、長期金利の対独スプレッ
ドが急上昇。スペインでは、住宅価格の高騰対策が
否決され、政府が議会を解散。現時点の世論調査
では、11月の総選挙後に極右勢力が政権入りする
可能性が高く、拡張的な財政運営への警戒が増大。
ユーロ圏景気概況：来年初にかけて減速する見通し

### Page 4

欧州
(株)日本総合研究所 欧州経済展望 2026年10月
◆個人消費は先行き減速へ
足元の英国景気は堅調 。８月の小売売上数量
は前月比＋0.5%と、高めの伸び。販売チャネル別
にみると、無店舗販売が持ち直したほか 、百貨店な
ど非専門店の販売も増加。
もっとも、先行きの個人消費は減速する見通し。イ
ンフレの加速が主因。８月の消費者物価は前年比
＋3.1％と、前月から伸びが拡大 。電力・ガス市場
局（Ofgem）は、足元の資源高を踏まえて 、
2027年１～３月期にかけて、エネルギー価格の上
限を段階的に引き上げる見込み 。これにより、インフ
レ率は前年比３％台後半まで上昇すると予想。
◆ＡＩ需要が企業の生産活動を下支え
足元の生産活動は 、業種によって二極化 。ＧＤ
Ｐに占めるシェアが大きいサービス業では、消費者向
けが低調である一方 、企業向けは堅調。旺盛なＡ
Ｉ需要を背景に、ＩＴ関連の情報サービス業などの
事業活動が活発化 。製造業でも 、コンピュータ ・電
子製品・光学機械などＡＩ関連の生産が拡大。

先行きの生産活動は底堅く推移する見通し 。個
人消費の減速を背景に消費者向けサービス業は伸
び悩む一方、ＡＩ関連の企業向けサービス業や製
造業の生産は今後も拡大すると予想。英国の民間
ＡＩ投資額は米国や中国に次ぐ規模であり 、フラン
スやドイツなど大陸欧州諸国よりもＡＩ需要による
生産押し上げ効果が大きくなる公算。
英国景気概況：家計部門は減速も、ＡＩ需要が下支え
